# AI Usage Log

## Entry 001 — Day 1 data collection script (src/data.py)
- Date: 2026-07-23
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): "Teach me step by step how to pull league-wide NBA data via nba_api and cache it to SQLite for Day 1."
- What was used: Full `src/data.py` — SQLite append-and-cache helper, retry wrapper for stats.nba.com timeouts, pull functions for player bio/advanced/base stats, shot locations, Synergy play-type shares, and `leaguedashlineups` (group_quantity 2/3/4/5) — plus a main runner looping over seasons. Endpoints and parameters were smoke-tested one at a time before being wired into the runner (caught and fixed a bug where `SynergyPlayTypes` silently returns 0 rows without `type_grouping_nullable='offensive'`).
- What was NOT AI-generated: Choice of feature categories, which endpoints map to the paper's method, season/scope decisions, and all research-question/method direction (per CLAUDE.md).

## Entry 002 — Align data pull to paper Table 3.3 spec
- Date: 2026-07-23
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): "Compare the current data pull against the 'Scouting Anyone' paper's Table 3.3 NBA feature spec (shot distribution buckets, %AST'd-2P/3P, play types) and fetch data matching it."
- What was used: (1) Corrected `PLAY_TYPES` to match the paper exactly (removed `Transition`, added `OffRebound`/putbacks). (2) Replaced the NBA.com 5ft-range shot-location pull with a new `pull_bref_shooting()` that scrapes Basketball-Reference's per-season "shooting" table — found by testing several B-Ref/nba_api endpoints live that it has the exact 0-3/3-10/10-16/16-3P buckets, corner-3 rate, average shot distance, and %AST'd-2P/3P all in one table, matching the paper's stated NBA.com + Basketball-Reference sourcing. (3) Fixed `pull_lineups()` to loop per-team (`team_id_nullable`) after discovering via live testing that `leaguedashlineups` silently truncates at exactly 2000 rows sorted by MIN descending — which was cutting off legitimate MIN≥100 2-man/3-man combos and structurally biasing the sample toward high-minute (star-heavy) pairs.
- What was NOT AI-generated: The decision to prioritize exact paper replication over a faster approximation, and the decision to build the Basketball-Reference scraper now rather than deferring it — both were explicit user choices (via AskUserQuestion) after the tradeoffs were presented.

## Entry 003 — Crash resilience + data-inspection tooling (src/inspect_data.py, src/data.py)
- Date: 2026-07-23
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): First, "run the data pull" surfaced a real crash — a rate-limit block mid-run with no way to resume without duplicating already-pulled data. Then a detailed prompt: "create a script that loads and displays all six dataset tables — inspect any documentation first, don't invent column definitions, mark unconfirmed ones clearly, use `rich`."
- What was used: Added `_already_saved`/`_pull_if_needed` (skip-if-cached resume logic) and longer backoff to `src/data.py`. Built `src/inspect_data.py` from scratch — a `rich`-based table/column-dictionary viewer plus a `--markdown` mode used to populate `README.md`. Verified no existing data dictionary existed in the repo before writing definitions (README.md and writeup/ were both empty). Also found and fixed a real encoding bug live: Basketball-Reference doesn't send a charset header, so `requests` was silently mangling accented names (e.g. "Jokić" → "JokiÄ") — fixed by forcing UTF-8 and re-pulling just the affected table.
- What was NOT AI-generated: The user supplied the full detailed spec for the inspection script (fields required, confidence-labeling rule, library choice) verbatim; it's preserved as this file's own module docstring per that request.

## Entry 005 — Day 2 feature table (src/features.py) + ADA toy validation (src/archetypes_model.py)
- Date: 2026-07-24
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): "Build the Day 2 feature table per paper Table 3.3, then run the ADA toy validation CLAUDE.md's pitfalls list calls for before committing to the `archetypes` package."
- What was used: `src/features.py` joins all 7 raw tables into one player-season feature table. Building it surfaced three real bugs, found and fixed live against actual data rather than assumed: (1) NBA.com tables key on numeric PLAYER_ID but the two B-Ref tables only have names — closed with a normalized-name join, verified at 99.6%+ match rate; (2) that join initially dropped Egor Demin, an actual 2025 Nets draft pick, because B-Ref's page uses a Cyrillic "ё" (U+0451) instead of the Latin "ë" NBA.com uses in his name — fixed by mapping common Cyrillic homoglyphs before normalizing, not by hardcoding his name; (3) confirmed empirically that `player_advanced.MIN` is a per-game average, not a season total, despite the table being pulled with `per_mode_detailed="Totals"` — using it for the >=300-minute filter would have silently zeroed out the entire dataset. `src/archetypes_model.py` fits `archetypes.ADA` on one season and verifies every resulting archetype is an exact-distance-zero match to a real player row, the property that distinguishes Archetypoid from standard Archetypal Analysis.
- What was NOT AI-generated: The >=300 min filter, the pooled (not per-season) z-scoring choice, and the decision to require ADA specifically (not AA) were all set in CLAUDE.md before this was built.

## Entry 007 — Step 3 direction change: drop the synergy-matrix approach, reframe as archetype-overlap diagnostic (src/step2_diagnostic_analysis.py)
- Date: 2026-07-26
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): Read the "Scouting Anyone" paper's description of three practical applications of a probabilistic archetype framework (diagnostic mismatch analysis / league-wide scouting / roster-construction balance) and, across several rounds of conversation, decided the synergy-matrix regression (`step2_synergy_matrix.py`) was hard to fully trust/explain and should be dropped in favor of application (1): "diagnostic analysis - identifying player combination mismatches in existing rosters," scoped to Brooklyn's own real 2025-26 on-court pairs. Final instruction: "新写 step2_diagnostic_analysis.py 然后可以在脚本内部再继续研究 按照你之前说的框架帮我写代码" (write a new file implementing the framework worked out in conversation).
- What was used: `src/step2_diagnostic_analysis.py` — loads Brooklyn's real 2-man on-court combos (`lineups` table, MIN>=50, 2025-26), attaches each side's Step-1 archetype recipe, computes a cosine-similarity "archetype overlap" score between the two recipe vectors (no regression, no fitted matrix), correlates overlap against observed net rating, and flags specific pairs as `mismatch` (high overlap + bottom-quartile net rating) or `complementary_success` (low overlap + top-quartile net rating) relative to Brooklyn's own real-pair distribution. Ran end-to-end against real data: 140 real BKN pairs (134 after the MIN>=300 recipe floor), weak negative correlation (pearson r=-0.118) in the hypothesized direction, with several real flagged pairs (e.g. Jalen Wilson+Drake Powell as a mismatch, both "D'Angelo Russell-type"; MPJ+Egor Dëmin as a complementary success) that look basketball-plausible on inspection. Also produced `figures/step2_overlap_vs_netrating.png` and `data/nets_pair_diagnostics.csv`.
- What was NOT AI-generated: The decision to drop the synergy-matrix/regression approach entirely, which paper application to reframe around, and the choice to scope this to Brooklyn's own played pairs rather than a league-wide or hypothetical-combo analysis — all reached by the user across the conversation, not proposed by Claude Code. `step2_synergy_matrix.py` is kept in the repo (not deleted) as visible prior work/thought process. Mikel Brown Jr.'s slot-query piece (Step 4) is explicitly deferred, not decided yet.

Bumped font sizes across the CSS block (body/caption text, h1-h4, table cells, metrics, tabs) and tightened `archetype_column_chart`'s `bargap`. Verified with `AppTest` (zero exceptions across page loads/player switches) and real Playwright screenshots.

## Entry 013 — Similarity-weighted league benchmark, replacing threshold-then-filter-on-outcome (src/step2_diagnostic_analysis.py)
- Date: 2026-07-27
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): the user identified two real problems with `find_comparables`/`league_benchmark_exposure` (Part E) directly, not prompted by Claude Code: (1) it returns an empty pool for thinner archetypes (why the League Benchmark tab was hidden), and (2) filtering the comparable pool to only positive-on-court-net-rating players is selection on the outcome variable - "successful players share this environment" is not "this environment causes success." Asked for `similarity_weighted_benchmark(player_id, recipes, k, season, oncourt=None, power=4.0)`: weight every player by Jensen-Shannon distance to the subject (not a hard threshold), weight=(1-JS)**power, return both an all-comparables and a positive-net-rating-only weighted baseline plus each one's effective sample size, and report how ESS changes across power in {2,4,8} before defaulting to 4.0 - not picked silently.
- What was used: Added `similarity_weighted_benchmark` using Jensen-Shannon distance (recipes are probability distributions - JS is the correct metric, unlike cosine/Euclidean) and `compare_to_benchmark` extended with an optional `baseline2` so both weighted baselines can be plotted together. Tested against Michael Porter Jr.'s real 2025-26 pool: power=2 -> ESS=192/432 (all) and 100.8/206 (positive-only); power=4 -> ESS=83.3/432 and 49.2/206; power=8 -> ESS=19.3/432 and 14.3/206 - defaulted to power=4.0 as the middle ground (power=2 is nearly half the league, too diffuse to call "similar"; power=8 concentrates on a sharp handful, similar thinness to the old hard-threshold approach). Separately confirmed the new function solves problem (1): Ben Saraf, a thinner-archetype player, gets a real non-empty benchmark (ESS≈73) where the old threshold approach could return zero comparables. A first naive implementation (recomputing every league player's own macro_archetype_exposure inside the same loop that varies `power`) took 90-200+ seconds per full run since that SQL-heavy step doesn't depend on `power` at all - refactored into `compute_all_player_exposures` (the genuinely expensive, season-only-dependent half, ~38s for ~430 players, meant to be cached once per season by callers) and `compute_style_pool_by_vector`/`compute_style_pool` (the cheap per-target JS-distance half) so a power sweep or an interactive UI slider costs ~0.02s once the season's exposures are cached. Also added `compute_style_pool_by_vector`/`compute_all_player_exposures`'s vector-based split (mirroring the existing find_similar_players/find_similar_players_by_vector real/hypothetical pattern) to support a hypothetical (non-fitted) recipe vector, anticipating Mikel Brown Jr.'s slot-query use in Entry 014.
- What was NOT AI-generated: identifying both real problems with the old approach, specifying Jensen-Shannon distance, and requiring the {2,4,8} power sensitivity check rather than accepting a default un-tested - all the user's own specification, not something Claude Code proposed unprompted.

## Entry 014 — Scouting page: JS-based similarity + interactive Brown slot-query hypothesis tool (src/portal.py, src/step2_diagnostic_analysis.py)
- Date: 2026-07-27
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): two changes to `render_scouting()`. (1) Replace cosine similarity in `find_similar_players`/`find_similar_players_by_vector` with Jensen-Shannon distance (cosine is insensitive to how concentrated a recipe's mass is), report similarity as 1-JS, and add AGE/MIN columns so the results answer "who plays like him but younger/cheaper," not just "who is similar." (2) Rewrite `render_brown_slot_query()` as an interactive hypothesis: `st.selectbox`+`st.slider` to hand-set a primary/secondary archetype and weight (default: on-ball scoring engine, his stated college identity per CLAUDE.md), fed through Entry 013's `similarity_weighted_benchmark` (what environment players of that style normally have) vs. `compute_team_archetype_profile` (what the Nets currently supply) to get a gap, plus a sensitivity check across several plausible archetype assumptions reporting whether the top gap is stable.
- What was used: `find_similar_players_by_vector` now computes JS distance per candidate (`1 - JS` reported, sorted descending, unchanged "higher = more similar" direction) and returns MIN alongside; `render_scouting` merges in AGE from `bio` and formats both to clean display strings (a first pass showed raw float64 precision like "21.0000"/"453.0000" - fixed with an explicit display copy, the same fix already applied to Diagnostic Analysis's Primary Teammates table). `render_brown_slot_query` was fully rewritten: two selectboxes + a weight slider build a k-dim hypothesis vector, run through `compute_style_pool_by_vector` (Entry 013, using a cached season-only exposure table so the slider is actually responsive - confirmed via a live Playwright session, dragging/reselecting re-renders in under a second) and `similarity_weighted_benchmark`, compared against the Nets' `compute_team_archetype_profile` to produce a "gap: typical environment for this style vs. Nets' current supply" diverging bar chart plus a computed one-sentence finding. The sensitivity sweep holds the user's chosen primary archetype fixed (that part - "on-ball scoring engine" - is CLAUDE.md's own stated college-identity note, not a free assumption) and varies only the two genuinely uncertain knobs (secondary archetype, weight ±15pp) across 4 variants, reporting "stable" (green) or "not stable" (yellow) based on whether the top-gap archetype label matches across all of them. Verified end-to-end with real data: for Brown's default hypothesis (70% Offensive Engine / 30% Shooting Specialist), the tool reports the Nets currently under-supply Offensive Engine/Inside Scoring Big/Mobile Big/Rim Protector-type environments relative to what similar-style players typically have, and over-supply Combo Guard (-10.8pp) - consistent with, and now quantifying, the roster's own previously-found Combo Guard redundancy - and confirmed the recommendation is stable across all 4 sensitivity variants tested.

## Entry 016 — Intro page rebuilt as a convex-hull geometric explainer (src/portal.py, src/precompute_hull_bases.py)
- Date: 2026-07-27
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): "Replace the Intro page in portal.py with a geometric explainer... Archetypoids are vertices of the convex hull of the player point cloud, and that is what the page should show." Full spec across the initial message and one same-turn follow-up: a scatter of all 433/434 players projected to 2D via PCA fit on the K archetypoid rows ONLY (explicitly not on all players, since a hull vertex in ~29-dim space need not sit on the boundary of a plane fit to everyone), reporting explained variance and how many of the K archetypoids actually land on the 2D ConvexHull of the projection ("say so" if fewer than 6), a K=4-10 slider backed by bases precomputed offline (a separate script, since refitting ADA live is far too slow), a hover template on every point, and - in the follow-up - a click-driven detail panel below the chart (`st.plotly_chart(..., on_select="rerun", selection_mode="points")`, checked the installed Streamlit version (1.56.0) supports it before using it) resolved by PLAYER_ID carried through customdata (never by name), reusing `render_profile_card`/`load_player_bio`/`archetype_column_chart` as-is, showing an archetypoid's own high/low feature z-scores when the clicked point IS one of the K, plus a fixed visual encoding per point group (archetypoids large/labeled/colored, Nets players their own color, everyone else small/low-opacity, the current selection ringed).
- What was used: `precompute_hull_bases.py` (new) fits K in {4,5,6,7,9,10} via step1's own `fit_basis_best_of`/`save_basis`/`match_archetypes_to_players` (no new fitting logic) and saves each to `data/hull_bases/k{K}/`; K=8 deliberately reuses the existing `data/basis_2025_26` fit rather than refitting, so the slider's K=8 always matches the rest of the portal. `compute_hull_projection(k)` fits `sklearn.PCA(n_components=2)` on `fit["basis"]` (the K archetypoid rows) and transforms everyone else onto that plane; archetypoids are matched back to rows in the freshly-loaded population by PLAYER_ID + closest MIN (not row-order, which isn't guaranteed stable across separate `load_population()` calls, and not PLAYER_ID alone, since a midseason-traded player can have more than one row - step1's own Part E note). Ran this for real on K=8: only 2/8 archetypoids land on the 2D hull of all 434 projected points (39%+30%=69% explained variance of the archetypoids' own spread) - a genuine, somewhat surprising finding, not a bug, and the `n_on_hull < 6` warning path was written specifically because this real number came in low. `build_hull_scatter` draws 4 layers (league/Nets/hull outline/archetypoids) plus a ring overlay for the selected point; `render_hull_detail_panel` reuses the three named functions verbatim and adds `archetype_feature_profile()` (a returning, not printing, mirror of step1's `describe_archetypes` body) for the "he IS an archetype" case. Caught and fixed two real bugs during Playwright verification, not just code review: (1) `Tim Hardaway Jr.`'s on-chart label read "Jr." (naive `name.split()[-1]`) - fixed with a small suffix-aware `_surname()` helper; (2) clicking a point updated the detail panel but the ring stayed on the previous selection for one extra render, because the figure is built from session_state BEFORE the click event is read - fixed with a single conditional `st.rerun()` that only fires when the click actually changes the selection (confirmed it settles rather than loops, via a live click-and-screenshot check). Verified the K slider against three states: K=8 (on disk from the start), K=6 (precomputed mid-session, correct exemplars/warning/EV% all updated), and K=9 (deliberately tested before its background precompute finished, to confirm the missing-basis guard warns cleanly instead of crashing).
- What was NOT AI-generated: the entire geometric framing (archetypoids as convex-hull vertices, not abstract cluster centers) and the PCA-must-be-fit-on-archetypoids-only correctness requirement, the decision to precompute K=4-10 offline rather than ever refit live, and every interaction/visual-encoding spec (hover vs. click, what the detail panel must show per case, which point group gets which treatment) - all specified directly by the user, not proposed by Claude Code. Also not AI: substituting `data/k_selection_2025_26.csv` for the literally-named `data/k_selection.csv` in the paired EV-vs-K chart - the latter reflects a different, superseded population (multi-season/higher MIN floor, per step1_archetypes_model.py's own Part A comment) and would have paired a mismatched curve with this chart; flagged here rather than silently followed literally.

New `top3_bar_chart` colors each bar by a fixed per-archetype hue (`ARCHETYPE_COLOR_PALETTE`, reusing `archetype_column_chart`'s own existing 6-color set rather than inventing a new palette) converted to rgba via a new `_hex_to_rgba` helper with alpha = `0.35 + 0.65 * min(pct/0.7, 1.0)` - so a 60% bar of a given archetype renders visibly darker/more saturated than a 20% bar of that same archetype, while the hue itself stays constant so the same archetype reads as the same color in every row. Verified visually: Ben Saraf's bars show 56%/39%/5% at correspondingly decreasing opacity in green/gold/coral; the multiselect correctly reflects that with only 5 real-data players there are just two distinct top-archetype values (Combo Guard, Traditional Playmaker) available to filter on.

## Entry 025 — src/step4_roster_construction.py: mixture-to-outcome regression (4-man primary, 2/3-man robustness, 5-man extrapolation check)
- Date: 2026-07-27
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): a gate result was already checked and handed in directly - 5-man lineups at MIN>=100 in 2025-26 number only 78, too few for a 10-predictor model, so build the new file around 4-man as the primary unit (915 rows) with 2-man/3-man as robustness. Full spec for six functions: `build_lineup_table(recipes, k, season, group_quantity, min_threshold=100)` - one row per lineup, mixture = mean of the n players' archetype recipes, entropy = exp(-sum p log p), sum_bpm, and 9 outcome columns with DEF_RATING and TM_TOV_PCT sign-flipped (DEF_RATING_INV, TM_TOV_PCT_INV) so everything reads "higher is better," joined to recipes/BPM by PLAYER_ID only, with the drop count reported; `fit_outcome_model` - WLS (weights=MIN) of outcome ~ sum_bpm + (K-1 archetype shares, dropping one as reference, defaulting to largest total exposure, always returned) + entropy, with bootstrap CIs from resampling TEAMS (not rows), 1000 draws; `cross_validate` - GroupKFold on TEAM_ABBREVIATION, baseline (sum_bpm only) vs. full, plus a boolean for whether full beats baseline; `granularity_comparison` - same model at n=2/3/4 side by side, with the reasoning stated that sum_bpm only controls for the n observed players so drift with n signals unobserved-teammate contamination; `five_man_extrapolation` - score the 78 five-man lineups with the 4-man model, weighted correlation, explicitly labeled as an extrapolation check and not validation; `predict_substitution` - shift delta of weight between two archetypes and report the predicted change. Explicit final instruction to print real numbers (n, reference archetype, weighted R^2, cross-validated R^2, full coefficient table with CIs, then the granularity comparison) for OFF_RATING at group_quantity=4, not just assert success.
- What was used: `_parse_group_ids` is imported from step2_diagnostic_analysis.py rather than reimplemented - that function (written earlier for Part D's exact-set lineup lookups) already is the n-agnostic generalization of step2_synergy_matrix's 2-only `_parse_pair_ids`; writing a third copy here would have duplicated logic the project already has. Player-ID -> recipe/BPM attachment explodes GROUP_ID into a long table (one row per lineup x player slot) and does a single vectorized merge, generalizing step2's attach_recipes/attach_quality_covariate from 2 slots to n. Reference archetype's "largest total exposure" was implemented as minutes-weighted total exposure (sum of MIN * mixture share across lineups) - a concrete, defensible reading given everything else in the model is MIN-weighted, stated explicitly since the spec left the exact definition open. The team-cluster bootstrap resamples whole teams with replacement (a sampled team contributes every one of its rows, more than once if resampled more than once) rather than a row-level bootstrap, per the spec's explicit reasoning that the same player appears in dozens of lineups. `granularity_comparison` fits the 4-man table first to pick ONE reference archetype and passes it explicitly into the 2-man/3-man fits, rather than letting each group_quantity pick its own argmax independently - a different reference per n would make "the arch_3_share coefficient" mean a different comparison at each row of the side-by-side table, defeating its purpose; this wasn't spelled out in the prompt and was a judgment call made to keep the requested comparison actually valid. `predict_substitution` omits sum_bpm as an argument (matching the exact given signature) because it cancels exactly out of the before/after difference when personnel is held fixed - verified with a synthetic mixture that the printed predicted-change number matches the coefficient arithmetic by hand (arch_6_share coefficient x -0.10 + entropy coefficient x its recomputed delta). Ran `phase_report()` end to end against the real 2025-26 data (data/basis_2025_26, K=8): 904/915 4-man lineups survive the recipe/BPM join (11 dropped), reference archetype resolves to arch_5 (Shooting Specialist), in-sample weighted R^2=0.2765, cross-validated R^2 is baseline=0.2269 vs. full=0.2196 - **full does NOT beat baseline for OFF_RATING at n=4, a real negative result, not a bug**; only arch_4_share's bootstrap CI excludes zero at n=4. The granularity comparison shows arch_4_share drifting from -5.36 (n=2) to -9.29 (n=3) to -15.59 (n=4), a monotonic drift in the direction the spec's own reasoning predicts as unobserved-teammate contamination shrinks. The 5-man extrapolation check (78 lineups, 1 dropped for a sub-300-minute Zach Edey) gives weighted corr(predicted, actual)=0.5557. Fixed one issue myself before reporting: the first `_entropy_vectorized` draft used `np.log(p, where=p>0)` without an `out=` array, which leaves numpy's output uninitialized at the masked (p<=0) positions - functionally harmless here since the outer `np.where` discards those entries regardless of their value, but fragile style and it throws a RuntimeWarning; replaced with `p * np.log(np.where(p>0, p, 1.0))`, which needs no masking at all since log(1)=0 pairs with p=0 to give exactly 0. Re-ran after the fix and confirmed byte-identical output.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the model specification itself (sum_bpm + K-1 shares + entropy, WLS weights=MIN), the three requirements behind it (sum_bpm to avoid star-detection, K-1 shares because of the collinearity with the intercept, entropy against the degenerate all-weight-on-one-archetype optimum), the team-cluster (not row-level) bootstrap requirement, the GroupKFold baseline-vs-full comparison and the requirement to flag when full doesn't beat baseline, the granularity-comparison reasoning connecting coefficient drift to unobserved-teammate count, the decision that 5-man is an extrapolation check rather than a fit target (the gate result), and predict_substitution's role as "the actionable output, sidesteps solving for an optimum" - all given directly. The already-checked gate result (78 five-man lineups, too few to fit) was handed in as a precondition, not derived in this session.

## Entry 026 — step4 follow-ups: cross_validate for all 9 outcomes, standardized coefficients in granularity_comparison, baseline correlation in five_man_extrapolation (src/step4_roster_construction.py)
- Date: 2026-07-27
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): three follow-ups, numbers-only, no interpretation, nothing built on top yet. (1) Run cross_validate() for all nine outcomes at group_quantity=4, one table: outcome, baseline CV R^2, full CV R^2, difference, full_wins. (2) In granularity_comparison(), add standardized coefficients (raw coef x SD of that share variable at that n) alongside the raw ones, and report each share's SD at n=2/3/4 - because raw coefficients aren't comparable across n (a 4-man mixture averages four recipes and so has less share variance than a 2-man one, mechanically inflating the raw coefficient at higher n with no contamination required), and if the standardized coefficients are flat while the raw ones drift, the arch_4_share contamination claim from Entry 025 has to be withdrawn. (3) In five_man_extrapolation(), also score the 78 lineups with the sum_bpm-only baseline and report its weighted correlation next to the full model's - the full model's 0.56 is uninterpretable alone.
- What was used: (1) done as a throwaway script, not a new permanent function in the module - the user's "do not build anything on top yet" was read as a scope limit on the file, not just on my response, so no `cross_validate_all_outcomes`-style helper was added; the script just loops `OUTCOME_COLUMNS` and calls the existing `cross_validate` once per outcome on the 904-row 4-man table. (2) Added `_weighted_std` next to the existing `_weighted_r2`/weighted-mean helpers (same population-weighted convention, weights=MIN, not bias-corrected) and computed each non-reference archetype share's weighted SD at n=2/3/4 inside `granularity_comparison`, then `std_coef = coef * sd` per cell; sum_bpm and entropy rows get NaN in the SD/standardized columns since the ask was specifically "each share's SD," not every regressor's. Caught and fixed my own bug before running: the first draft cached the 4-man table via `lineup_tables.get(gq) or build_lineup_table(...)` - `or` forces a truthiness check, and a non-empty pandas DataFrame's `__bool__` raises `ValueError` ("truth value of a DataFrame is ambiguous"), so this would have crashed on the very first (n=4) iteration; replaced with an explicit `if gq not in lineup_tables` membership check. (3) Extracted the weighted-correlation logic (previously inlined once in `five_man_extrapolation`) into a shared `_weighted_corr` helper since it's now needed twice, then added a second fit inside `five_man_extrapolation`: a sum_bpm-only WLS model trained on the same 4-man table the full model used, scored on the 78 five-man lineups the same way, reported alongside the full model's correlation. This changed the function's returned dict keys from `weighted_corr`/`predicted` to `weighted_corr_full`/`weighted_corr_baseline`/`predicted_full`/`predicted_baseline` - a breaking rename, acceptable since nothing else in the codebase consumes this function's output yet.
- Real numbers from re-running phase_report() plus the three follow-ups, OFF_RATING @ group_quantity=4 unless noted: (1) cross_validate across all 9 outcomes - full beats baseline for DEF_RATING_INV (+0.0307), NET_RATING (+0.0169), REB_PCT (+0.0270), OREB_PCT (+0.0666), AST_PCT (+0.2629); full does NOT beat baseline for OFF_RATING (-0.0073), EFG_PCT (-0.0041), TM_TOV_PCT_INV (-0.0272), PACE (-0.0121). (2) OFF_RATING's arch_4_share raw coefficient goes -5.36 (n=2, SD=0.0916) -> -9.29 (n=3, SD=0.0738) -> -15.59 (n=4, SD=0.0618); standardized, that's -0.49 -> -0.69 -> -0.96 - still drifting, not flat, so the SD-scaling explanation alone does not account for the full raw drift on this particular share. Other shares vary in how much of their raw drift survives standardization (e.g. arch_1_share's raw coefficient is fairly stable, -6.85/-6.55/-4.52, but its standardized version drifts more, -0.67/-0.51/-0.28, moving in the opposite direction). Full 7-share x 3-n table of raw coef, SD, and standardized coef was printed and returned in `comparison_table`. (3) five_man_extrapolation for OFF_RATING: full model weighted corr(pred, actual)=0.5557 vs. sum_bpm-only baseline=0.5232 (78 lineups, 1 dropped for sub-300-minute Zach Edey, same as Entry 025).
- What was NOT AI-generated: which three follow-ups to run, the exact standardized-coefficient formula (coef x SD, not a full both-sides standardization), the reasoning for why raw coefficients aren't comparable across n, and the requirement that the baseline correlation be reported specifically to make the full model's number interpretable - all given directly. The interpretation of what the resulting numbers mean (e.g., whether the contamination claim actually gets withdrawn) was explicitly left to the user ("report numbers only, do not build anything on top yet"), not decided here.

## Entry 028 — new src/hull_callout_chart.py: anatomical-diagram leader-line callouts for the 8 archetypoids + custom hover cards for archetypoids/Nets players, replacing build_hull_scatter entirely (src/hull_callout_chart.py, src/portal.py)
- Date: 2026-07-27
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): full, highly detailed spec for a redesign of the Intro page's hull scatter. Two parts. (A) Replace the archetypoid dots' on-chart presence with "anatomical-diagram-style" leader-line callouts: each of the 8 archetypoid vertices gets its own identity color (8 named hex values, assigned in CLOCKWISE order around the vertices as plotted, from a given palette), a leader line that goes vertex -> knee (46px along the vertex's own direction from the centroid) -> end (a further 40px purely horizontal, direction depending on which side of the centroid the vertex is on), a label anchored 8px past the end (left-aligned if the line went right, right-aligned if it went left), two-line text (name 16px semibold ink-colored, role 14px uppercase in the vertex's identity color), decluttering (if two same-side labels land within 26px vertically, push them apart and drag the leader line's knee to match), and canvas padding wide enough (~310px each side) that no label - specifically named: Shai Gilgeous-Alexander's long name and "Rim Protector / Roll Man"'s long role - clips. The archetypoid list column beside the chart is deleted outright. (B) Nets players become plain black dots (visually one tier below the colored archetypoid vertices, one tier above the grey background cloud), with NO static label/photo by default - only a custom hover card (photo + name + position/number + top 1-2 weighted archetypes, colors matching each archetype's own identity color) driven by real plotly_hover/plotly_unhover JS events (explicitly not Plotly's native hovertemplate, which the user already knew can't hold an image), plus an enlarge+halo+dim-all-others interaction on the hovered dot. Implementation constraints given directly: Plotly shapes/annotations for the leader lines/labels (not hardcoded per-K), one consistent coordinate system, a reusable geometry function taking vertices+labels+colors and emitting shapes+annotations, a local player_id->image-path cache with an initials-fallback for missing photos, and instructions to export both a default-state and a hover-state PNG for review before considering it done.
- What was used: Built an entirely new module (hull_callout_chart.py) rather than extending build_hull_scatter, since custom JS hover events (plotly_hover/plotly_unhover) require the chart to run inside a real HTML/JS page - st.markdown doesn't execute <script> tags (hit this exact wall earlier this session building the roster table's sort feature) and st.plotly_chart has no on-hover callback in its Python API, so this had to be Plotly.js loaded from CDN inside st.components.v1.html(), not a go.Figure handed to st.plotly_chart. The leader-line algorithm (compute_leader_callouts) is implemented literally as specified, entirely in PIXEL space (since 46/40/8/26px are screen-space quantities, not data units) with a fixed figure width/height and zero margins so the data<->pixel conversion used for that algorithm stays valid, converting the final geometry back to data coordinates once for the actual Plotly shapes/annotations. Colors are assigned by computing the centroid of the 8 archetypoid marker positions (the same positions already used for the dots, all_2d[archetype_row_idx[a]] - not basis_2d, which is a separate abstract-basis projection only used for the dashed hull outline) and sorting by descending atan2 angle around it (clockwise, since y grows upward) - checked live that scipy's own ConvexHull.vertices order (used elsewhere for the hull outline) is counterclockwise, and that one archetype (Bez Mbeng) isn't even a vertex of that particular hull, so centroid-angle sorting was used instead of reusing outline_idx, since it handles every vertex including interior ones. Headshots are cached locally (data/headshot_cache/{player_id}.png, downloaded once) and embedded as base64 data URIs rather than hot-linked - checked earlier this session that Plotly's own add_layout_image silently fails to render a remote URL under kaleido's static export (no network access in that headless renderer, confirmed via a side-by-side data-URI-vs-remote-URL test), so a data URI is the generally more robust choice, not just a "local path" technicality. Jersey number/position for Nets players (needed for the hover card, not present in any pulled DB table - player_bio/player_base have no such column) came from the ESPN roster page already fetched and verified in Entry 027, not re-guessed. Nets/archetypoid position, weighted-archetype text, and colors are all read live from `proj`/`labels` at render time - nothing about a specific K or specific player is hardcoded, so the same function works if K changes.
- Bugs found and fixed during verification (all via Playwright, not eyeballed): (1) `scattergl` (WebGL) traces didn't visually reflect per-point marker.size/opacity changes from Plotly.restyle/animate even though the underlying trace data updated correctly - switched all three traces to plain `scatter` (SVG), which is more than fast enough at ~450 total points. (2) After switching to `scatter`, hovering exactly over a Nets marker's own rendered screen position fired `plotly_hover` for the WRONG trace (curveNumber 0, the background population, at a different point) - root-caused by logging `evt.points[0].curveNumber` live: Plotly's "closest point" hover detection compares raw pixel distance to point centers across ALL traces and doesn't account for trace stacking/marker size, so a different real player who happens to land within a few pixels of a Nets player in the 2D projection can steal its hover event. Fixed by dropping background points within 14px of any Nets/archetypoid marker before building the background trace (410 -> 363 points) - those points are already fully hidden under the foreground marker anyway, so this is a hover-reliability fix with no visual side effect, not a data change. (3) The chart's fixed 1400px design width clipped in the real app (Streamlit's actual content column measured live at 1008px in a 1500px browser window, 1428px at 1920px - genuinely responsive to browser size, not a fixed Streamlit constant) even though it looked perfect in the standalone test file at a fixed 1450px viewport - fixed with a CSS `transform: scale()` on a wrapper div, computed at runtime from `document.documentElement.clientWidth`, so Plotly still lays out/hit-tests against the full 1400x700 box internally (transform changes paint, not layout - none of the precomputed pixel-space geometry needed to change) while the visible chart shrinks or modestly grows to fit whatever width Streamlit actually gives it; the hover-card div, positioned from raw untransformed clientX/clientY as a viewport-fixed sibling (not a scaled child), needed no adjustment at all.
- Verified before wiring into portal.py: unit-tested the geometry function standalone (confirmed monotonic decluttering with deliberately clustered synthetic points); confirmed clockwise color order and headshot caching (real download + cache hit + two separate not-found paths: invalid PLAYER_ID and no PLAYER_ID at all) against real data; screenshotted the standalone HTML default state and programmatically checked all 8 label bounding boxes against the canvas (0 clipped, including the two specifically-named long labels); tested hover (card content, enlarge/halo/dim-others via direct marker-array inspection, not just visually), unhover, and a rapid same-jump mouse move (no intermediate steps) to confirm no stale card survives fast movement; re-verified all of the above a second time inside the actual live Streamlit page (not just the standalone file) after the scale-to-fit fix, since the clipping bug above only showed up there. Two final PNGs (default state, hover state on Day'Ron Sharpe showing his real photo/position/weighted archetypes) captured from the live app and handed over as requested.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the entire leader-line-callout concept and its exact geometry algorithm, the 8-color palette and every pixel constant, the hover-card content/style spec, the Nets-dot interaction spec (enlarge/halo/dim-others), and the local-image-cache-with-fallback requirement - all given in full, precise detail directly. The three bugs above (scattergl restyle, hover-stealing background point, real-app clipping) and their fixes were not part of the spec - found and resolved during the verification pass this entry's instructions explicitly required ("导出一张PNG给我看默认状态...再截一张hover状态的图").

That silently implies a Nets-context number when it's actually a different team's number. Fixed by nulling net_on too whenever there's zero BKN floor time (added a `has_bkn_floor_time` column to the function's output so callers can explain the gap explicitly rather than let the row just look incomplete) - re-verified against real data that exactly these 3 players (and only these 3) now show NaN across all three columns, and confirmed nothing else consumes `screening_table`'s column set in a way an added column would break.
(1) Reused the exact same fig.add_layout_image approach already verified live in a browser for the Intro page's hull scatter (Entry 028/029), including keeping a fully transparent same-position marker trace underneath purely so Streamlit's `on_select="rerun"` click-to-select still has a real click target (layout images aren't clickable on their own) - and reused `hull_callout_chart.get_headshot_data_uri` for the photos themselves (local cache + fallback) rather than a third separate image-loading implementation. The four quadrant meanings are dashed-bordered background rectangles (`fig.add_shape`, `layer="below"`) with a low-opacity label in each one's own far corner - their shared inner edges sit exactly where the old `add_vline`/`add_hline` median dividers were, so those were dropped as redundant. Switched the axis ranges from auto to explicit (padded past the real data extent) since the quadrant rectangles and the image sizing both need a known, fixed range to size against.
(2) and (4) together: extracted a new shared `_build_sortable_table_html(table_id, columns, rows_cells)` from the Intro roster table's inline sort-table code (previously only usable by that one table) and rewrote the Screening table to call it with its own columns (Photo/Player/MIN/Top Archetype/Concentration/Mismatch/Net On/Net Off/Net Diff) and a leftmost photo column, removing the old `st.selectbox`+`st.checkbox` sort UI entirely. Caught a real bug of my own in this refactor before it shipped: the first call site passed `table_id="roster-table"` (a hyphen), which gets interpolated directly into the generated JS as `sortTable_roster-table` / `sortState_roster-table` - both invalid JS identifiers (a hyphen reads as subtraction), which would have silently broken the Intro table's sort with no Python-side error at all, only discoverable by actually running the JS. Confirmed the exact failure with a minimal Node repro before fixing it (changed to `roster_table`, no hyphen) and added a `table_id.isidentifier()` guard directly inside `_build_sortable_table_html` so this class of bug fails loudly in Python next time instead of shipping silently.
- Verified end to end via Playwright against the real running app, not just Python-side smoke tests: navigated to the Diagnostic Analysis page, waited out the ~35s exposure-cache spinner, confirmed 13 layout images (16 recipe-eligible players minus the 3 with no net_diff, matching exactly), 4 quadrant shapes, 4 quadrant annotations; clicked a specific player's photo (Drake Powell) on the chart and confirmed Layer 2 below updated to show him (click-to-select survived the redesign); clicked the "Mismatch" column header inside the screening table's iframe and confirmed both the ascending sort order and the ▲ indicator rendered correctly, with Randle/Ellis/Wagner's "-" rows correctly interspersed by mismatch value rather than pinned anywhere special.

The two bugs caught during implementation (the layout-image hover/click-target pattern's known constraints, and the hyphenated JS identifier) were found during this entry's own verification pass, not part of the request.

## Entry 031 — hover values capped at ~3 decimals app-wide (a real Plotly.js format-spec bug, not a display preference), plus median-line labels on the quadrant scatter (src/portal.py)
- Date: 2026-07-28
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): "在Hover里显示的value 最多只保留3位小数 不要出现那种很长的数字串" (hover values should keep at most 3 decimal places, no long digit strings) - triggered by the Layer 1 quadrant chart's own tooltip showing "net diff=-9.000000000000002" from the previous entry's screenshot. Mid-turn follow-up: "screen散点图中也标注虚线代表的意思" (label what the dashed line means on the screening scatter too).
- What was used: reproduced the bug in isolation before touching any app code, since the reported number was surprising given the hovertemplate already specified `%{y:+.1f}` (which should force exactly 1 decimal). A minimal two-point Plotly.js test confirmed: (1) an explicit fixed-decimal format spec (`:+.1f`) on a float carrying floating-point noise (e.g. -9.000000000000002, the kind net_diff = NRTG_ON - NRTG_OFF produces from ordinary subtraction) renders the FULL unrounded number, silently ignoring the format spec; (2) the exact same noisy value with NO explicit decimal spec (bare `%{y:+}`) renders cleanly ("-9") - Plotly's default number-to-string conversion uses JS's own shortest-round-trip algorithm, which the fixed-precision code path apparently bypasses. This means the fix has to be "round the value in Python before it reaches the trace, then use a bare/sign-only format spec" - a `.1f`-style spec after rounding wasn't tested as necessary once the bare-spec approach was confirmed reliable, and removing the explicit spec entirely avoids depending on a code path already shown to misbehave. Audited every hovertemplate/px.bar default in portal.py and applied this pattern to each value with any realistic floating-point exposure (subtractions, divisions, weighted means): `build_quadrant_chart` (mismatch_score to 3dp, net_diff/MIN rounded), `lift_bar_chart` (lift to 2dp, exposure mass rounded), `diverging_bar` (x_col rounded to 2dp inside the shared function itself, fixing its 3 callers - including Layer 2's "B" chart, which had no hovertemplate override at all and was silently showing px.bar's raw-float default hover, an separate instance of the same class of bug from a different cause) plus each of the 3 callers' own extra hovertemplate overrides, and the Scouting page's similarity bar chart. Left percentage-format specs (`.0%`/`.1%`) on non-subtraction values (raw archetype-membership fractions, precomputed explained-variance) untouched - not implicated in the repro and not derived via subtraction, so lower risk, and no evidence of an actual problem there.
Median labels: added two short annotations to build_quadrant_chart - "MEDIAN MISMATCH (value)" centered at the top of the vertical divider, "MEDIAN NET DIFF (value)" rotated -90° along the right edge of the horizontal divider - the only two spots on the frame the four quadrant-meaning corner labels (added in Entry 030) don't already occupy.
- Verified: re-ran build_quadrant_chart against real data and printed the trace's own y-array directly - confirmed 3.6/-2.5/4.4/8.0/6.2/-9.0/... (clean 1-decimal values, not 16-digit noise) for all 13 plotted players. Restarted the live app and hovered the exact same point as the original bug report (E.J. Liddell this time, same underlying data) - tooltip now reads "net diff=+3.6", confirmed via a cropped screenshot of the live tooltip, not just the underlying data. Confirmed the two new median annotations render as "MEDIAN MISMATCH (0.152)" and "MEDIAN NET DIFF (+2.9)" in the live screenshot.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the report itself and the 3-decimal ceiling - given directly. The root-cause diagnosis (isolating exactly which combination - explicit fixed-decimal spec + floating-point noise - triggers the bug, versus assuming "Plotly hover is just broken" and reaching for a workaround without understanding why) was this entry's own investigation, not handed in.

## Entry 036 — Layer 2 detailed-stats follow-up redesign: shot distance distribution as a real half-court zone chart, play-type usage as a pie, Base stats added alongside Advanced (src/portal.py)
- Date: 2026-07-28
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): "改造这个图 shot distance distribution使用court作为背景图画出来 play type usage 使用pie chart Box Score rates也包含基础的Base stats not those advanced" (redesign this chart: draw shot distance distribution over a court background image, make play type usage a pie chart, and have Box score rates also include basic Base stats, not just the advanced ones) - a follow-up to Entry 034's bar-chart version of the same section.
- What was used: `_court_line_segments()` + `build_court_shot_chart()` draw a real NBA half-court (regulation feet, hoop at the origin - baseline, backboard, rim, restricted area, lane, free-throw circle, three-point line with correct straight corners + 23.75ft arc, half-court circle/line) as one Scatter line trace with None-gap segments, with the 5 distance-bucket shares rendered as concentric hoop-centered bands underneath (theta-swept Scatter polygons, `fill="toself"`, `hoveron="fills"` for hover, plus an always-visible `%`+label annotation per band) - concentric because the underlying feature (`% of FGA by Distance_X-Y`) is pure straight-line distance from the hoop with no corner/wing split in the source data at all, so a radially-symmetric band is the honest shape for what the number actually measures, not a fabricated approximation of the true zone geometry; the real 3PT line is drawn on top so the stylized 16-3P/3PT boundary (fixed at 23.75ft) can be visually compared to where the line actually sits (22ft in the corners). Passes the FULL 5-bucket set (not a top-N subset like Entry 034's bar version) since a spatial court chart with a zone omitted would just show blank, unlabeled court. `build_playtype_pie()` shows the top-3 play types plus one "Other" slice.
Box score rates split into two separate single-row tables under new subheadings "Base stats" (GP/MIN/PTS/REB/AST/STL/BLK/FG%/3P%/FT%, from the already-existing `load_player_base_stats` - reused verbatim, not a new query) and "Advanced" (the pre-existing TS%/USG%/etc. table from Entry 034), both in the same `_build_sortable_table_html` style.
- Bug caught and fixed before shipping, found while sanity-checking the pie's own numbers rather than assumed correct: the first "Other" implementation summed only the 6 tracked-but-not-top-3 PLAYTYPE_ columns, which was 0.0 for a player whose only nonzero tracked play types were exactly his top 3 - checked `full_features[playtype_cols].sum(axis=1)` league-wide and found the 9 tracked Synergy play-type columns alone average only ~69% of a player's total possessions (max 91%, some players 0%) since this project's feature set carries 9 of Synergy's full play-type taxonomy and excludes categories like Transition/Misc entirely (a real, previously-documented feature-selection choice, not missing data) - meaning the naive "Other" undercounted real usage and the pie would have silently summed to well under 100% of the player's actual shot diet. Fixed by computing `other_pct = max(0.0, 1.0 - sum(all 9 tracked shares))` (the true gap to a full 100%) instead, and added a one-line caption under the pie stating "Other" covers both his smaller tracked play types and untracked Synergy categories, so the number isn't misread as "just doesn't do much else."
- Verified: unit-tested both chart builders directly against E.J. Liddell's real row before touching the browser (confirmed 6 traces/5 annotations for the court chart matching the 5 real distance shares, and pie values 31.4/10.9/8.8/48.9 summing to 100.0); rendered both to standalone PNGs and inspected them (court: recognizable half-court with all 5 zones, real 3PT line visible on top of the stylized zone boundary; pie: correct percentages, "Other" no longer near-zero). Restarted the live app, expanded "Detailed stats" via Playwright, and screenshotted the full section - confirmed Base stats and Advanced render as two separate real-data tables, the court chart shows all 5 zones with real headshot-page data, and the pie matches the standalone test. Hovered the court chart's gold "3-10 ft" band directly and confirmed the tooltip reads "3-10 ft: 27%" - fills-based hover detection works, not just the always-on text labels.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the three-part redesign spec (court background, pie chart, add Base stats) - given directly. The "Other" undercounting bug and the feature-set-coverage discovery behind it were found during this entry's own verification, not part of the request.
