I answered both with real numbers/code rather than reassurance: ran the actual agree/disagree logic across all 16 roster players and found it's 7 agree / 9 disagree, not "always disagree" (the default-shown player and a few marquee names the user likely checked - Liddell, MPJ, Randle - all happen to be disagree, which was probably the source of the impression); and confirmed the second point was correct - Section B fully decomposes the x-axis's mismatch_score, but Section C's teammate-pair data structurally can never decompose net_diff (on/off), since it only ever covers minutes the focal player himself was ON the floor. The user then proposed picking a different y-axis instead of patching over the gap; I recommended NRTG_ON specifically (plain on-court net rating, already Section C's own baseline) over other candidates, checked its noise properties against real 2025-26 BKN team_onoff data before recommending it (std(NRTG_ON)=3.11, std(NRTG_OFF)=3.33, std(NRTG_DIFF)=4.80 - a difference of two noisy numbers is noisier than either alone, so switching to NRTG_ON is a noise IMPROVEMENT, not a tradeoff), and flagged that the quadrant labels' causal framing (built around the on/off comparison) would need rework. The user then said "那就改用NRTG_ON 帮我把diagostic的页面重整" (switch to NRTG_ON, redo the page around it).
- What was used: `screening_table` (step2_diagnostic_analysis.py) rewritten to source `net_rating` directly from `oncourt` (player_advanced.NET_RATING via load_player_oncourt_netrating) instead of team_onoff's on/off split - this is a single league-wide table already covering every player regardless of team, so the whole per-team on/off-pull mechanism (onoff_by_team, has_bkn_floor_time) became unnecessary and was removed; `team_abbr` (from a single player_base query) replaces `onoff_team` for the same "which team does this number describe" transparency tag on the 3 cross-team players (Randle/Ellis/Wagner), without needing team_onoff at all. `load_player_onoff_split` itself was NOT deleted (team_onoff still holds real pulled data from Entry 032) - just noted as no longer called from here.
`build_quadrant_chart`/`render_screening_table`/`render_layer1_screening` (portal.py): every `net_diff` reference switched to `net_rating`; y-axis title changed to "on-court net rating"; median annotation to "MEDIAN NET RATING"; the three-column Net On/Net Off/Net Diff table collapsed into one "Net Rating" column (same cross-team gold-tag mechanism, applied once instead of three times per row). Bottom-right quadrant label renamed from "ENVIRONMENT PROBLEM" to "FIT PROBLEM" - the old label specifically leaned on the on/off comparison ("team is worse WITHOUT him" = a context claim), which plain NRTG_ON can't support as confidently since it can't separate "atypical fit dragging him down" from "he just isn't playing well right now" the way an on/off swing at least partially could; the other three quadrant labels' logic didn't depend on the on/off comparison and were left unchanged. The "zero BKN floor time" caption was reworded from a missing-data workaround into a plain transparency note, since player_advanced already had a real number for all 3 cross-team players with no gap to fill.
- Verified: ran the real 16-player agree/disagree tabulation and the real BKN team_onoff std comparison directly against the DB before recommending anything (not asserted from memory or general priors). After implementing, compiled both files, smoke-tested `screening_table`+`build_quadrant_chart` directly (confirmed real net_rating values matching player_advanced, team_abbr correctly flagging MIN/CLE/ORL for the 3 traded players, chart structure unchanged in count - 1 trace/9 annotations/5 shapes - just relabeled), restarted the live app, and screenshotted Layer 1 end to end: y-axis reads "on-court net rating," quadrant labels show the renamed "FIT PROBLEM," the median annotation reads "MEDIAN NET RATING (-8.4)," cross-team gold tags still render under the right photos, and the screening table shows a single real-valued "Net Rating" column with the same 3 gold-tagged rows.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the two methodology critiques that started this entry (B/C always disagreeing, in retrospect a sampling impression not a framework property; and B/C only covering "part of" what the scatter shows, which held up) - both the user's own observations. The metric switch itself (NRTG_ON over other candidates, over patching the asymmetry with a caveat) was a collaborative decision across several turns, grounded in real noise numbers I ran before endorsing it - not handed to me as a foregone conclusion, and not something I proposed unprompted.

## Entry 040 — new src/build_stints.py: a full-season (1230 games, real) directed offensive/defensive stint table for a skill-weighted archetype-RAPM model, built from scratch on nba_api's v3 endpoints after pbpstats turned out to be broken for 2025-26 (src/build_stints.py, STINTS_README.md, requirements.txt)
- Date: 2026-07-28
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): a full, detailed spec - "You are a senior sports-data engineer. Build a runnable Python pipeline that collects and preprocesses NBA 2025-26 regular-season possession data into a clean, regression-ready 'stint' table for a skill-weighted archetype-RAPM model... prefer pbpstats... disk-caching + retry/backoff + resumable... output schema game_id/date/stint_id/offense_team/defense_team/home_off/off_p1..5/def_p1..5/off_possessions/off_points/y_off... validation report" - followed by "帮我抓数据 并且记得和我现有的数据不要conflict" (pull the data, and don't conflict with my existing data). This is a standalone pipeline for a possible future modeling extension (see the earlier same-session data-readiness audit and archetype-RAPM planning discussion) - separate from this project's main archetype/synergy/portal codebase.
- What was used: **pbpstats was tried first** (the prompt's own stated preference) - installed it, patched its stale bundled HTTP headers (a live 2014-era UA + `http://` referer that gets silently empty-body'd by current stats.nba.com bot filtering), and confirmed via a raw request (4 retries, 60s timeout each) that its `stats_nba` provider's `boxscoretraditionalv2` dependency **no longer serves data for 2025-26 at all** - nba_api itself raises `BoxScoreTraditionalV2 is deprecated... Data is no longer being published... as of the 2025-26 NBA season`, and pbpstats' last PyPI release (1.3.11, 2024-04-17) predates that deprecation by over a year. Presented this finding plus 3 options (build from scratch on modern endpoints / search for another library / pause); the user chose to build from scratch on nba_api's `BoxScoreTraditionalV3`/`PlayByPlayV3` (both verified live: fast, complete, real 2025-26 data).
Built a from-scratch stint/possession parser: on-court lineup tracking (period-1 starters from the box score's own starter convention; substitutions applied by resolving BOTH named players in "SUB: A FOR B" to ids and using CURRENT ON-COURT STATE - not word order - to determine direction, after finding live that NBA's own text has no fixed in/out convention; name matching reused this project's own `_normalize_name` for diacritic mismatches and added first-initial-prefix handling for same-family-name teammates like "F. Wagner"/"M. Wagner"); possession-ending logic (made shot/turnover/last-of-trip free throw end a possession immediately or via the following rebound event for misses, matching the assignment's explicit requirement to use a real event-based count, not the FGA+0.44·FTA estimate); directed-row construction (2 rows per stint, one per team as offense, each carrying both sides' 5-man lineups, home/away, possessions, points, y_off).
Found and fixed, in order, three real bugs during iterative real-game testing (not assumed correct from the design alone): (1) a substitution-direction cascade when the OLD word-order assumption was wrong, fixed via the state-based resolution above; (2) unlogged between-period lineup changes (a player can reappear next period with zero substitution event recorded for his return at all) - found by cross-checking every shot/rebound/turnover/foul's own personId against tracked state and finding a period-2 rebound credited to a player still marked "subbed out since period 1" - fixed via `_period_opening_five()`, which re-derives each period's true opening five from directly-observed action-attributed players (walking the WHOLE period, not stopping at the first substitution, since a genuine early sub can precede still-later direct evidence for a true opener) rather than trusting the carried-forward guess blindly; (3) ~3% of total points silently disappearing, found via a real box-score cross-check (summed `off_points` vs. true final score) that the user asked me to actually run rather than accept a vague "internally consistent" placeholder - root-caused to NBA rules allowing a substitution between free throws of a multi-shot trip, where a non-last made FT scores real points without ending a possession by this file's own rules, and if a stint boundary lands inside that trip the whole row (points included) was being dropped since it had 0 counted possessions; fixed by flooring to 1 possession instead of dropping the row when a side scored real points with an otherwise-zero possession count.
Also found and fixed a narrower bug on the full run itself: 5/1230 games failed outright because both teams' schedule rows carried an IDENTICAL `MATCHUP` string (a per-row perspective assumption that isn't universally true), leaving neither row identifiable as home - fixed by parsing the home tricode directly out of the "X @ Y" text instead.
- Verified at increasing scale, checkpointing with the user twice before committing to the full run (given the "riskier lift" they'd explicitly chosen): 2-3 games by hand-tracing every substitution against real play-by-play; 10 games with a full validation report (pace realistic, 5v5 structural check clean) - first checkpoint, user chose to keep debugging the ~28% substitution-ambiguity rate rather than proceed; after the period-opening fix cut it to ~6% and self-correcting (not compounding), second checkpoint, user approved the full run. Ran all 1230 games in the background (real, live-pulled, resumable - manifest-tracked, per-game raw-response caching). **Final validation, the full season, not a sample**: 1230/1230 games parsed (100%, after the MATCHUP fix); 75,587 total directed rows; **1230/1230 games (100.0%) exactly match their real final score** (this was only ~5.7% exact before the free-throw-trip fix, off by a mean of ~6.75 combined points/game - re-verified this closed to exact across the entire season, not just a sample, before considering it done); every row passes the 5-distinct-offense + 5-distinct-defense player-id check with zero exceptions; all 30 teams land at 90.6-98.4 offensive possessions/game (typical NBA pace ~95-105/game - a residual small undercount on the low end for some teams, documented honestly rather than hidden, tied to the same ~6% substitution ambiguity affecting the possession-count denominator specifically, not points).
- What was NOT AI-generated: the full pipeline spec (stint definition, directed-row schema, real event-based possession requirement, caching/resumability/retry requirements, validation checks to run) - given directly and in detail. Every checkpoint decision (build from scratch vs. search further vs. pause; keep debugging vs. accept ~28% and run; run the full season now after ~6%) was the user's own, made after I presented concrete findings/tradeoffs, not decided unilaterally. The "don't conflict with existing data" constraint (own directory, own manifest, never touching `data/nets_synergy.db`) was explicit. The three root-cause bugs above and the MATCHUP bug were found during this entry's own testing/validation discipline (including the user's own push to actually run a real score cross-check rather than accept a vague internal-consistency claim), not handed in as known issues.

## Entry 041 — quantified what the possession-pace gap actually correlates with (substitution frequency, team archetype composition), documented in build_stints.py's own module docstring (src/build_stints.py, STINTS_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-28
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): "Could you mark it down at the start of build_stints.py please or you can check 每队的 pace 缺口 和 换人频率 / 某些原型占比 有没有明显相关" (document it at the start of build_stints.py, or you could check whether each team's pace gap correlates noticeably with substitution frequency / certain archetype shares) - given after Entry 040 reported the pace gap (90.6-98.4 vs. a loosely-cited "typical ~95-105") as a documented-but-unexplained limitation. Chose to actually run the check rather than just document a guess.
- What was used: pulled nba_api's own official `LeagueDashTeamStats` (Advanced) PACE/POSS for all 30 teams, 2025-26 - a real, direct ground truth this project hadn't compared against yet (Entry 040's "~95-105" was a generic rule of thumb, not this season's own real numbers). Every one of the 30 teams is undercounted vs. this real number, by 3.5-7.1 possessions/game (mean 5.4, ~5.4% relative) - a systematic gap, not per-team noise, confirmed by checking all 30, not a sample. Computed real per-team substitution frequency (counted directly from the already-cached play-by-play, not estimated) and real per-team archetype composition (this project's own K=8 recipes.csv, MIN-weighted mean, joined by team abbreviation - all 30 teams matched) and correlated both against the true gap (real official pace minus this pipeline's own computed pace): substitution frequency r≈-0.40 to -0.42 (moderate, right-signed - more subs, bigger gap, real evidence the documented ~6% substitution-ambiguity limitation is a genuine contributor); team archetype share's strongest correlate is arch_7 (Mbeng-type: low USG%/BPM, high STL%, PnR-adjacent) at r≈+0.63 (stronger than substitution frequency, but a plausible-not-confirmed story given only 30 data points - steal-heavy/scrambly playstyles may create more broken-play substitution clusters). Noted honestly that even the single best team (Boston) still has a +3.5-possession gap, so substitution ambiguity is very likely not the whole explanation - flagged 3 specific possession-ending scenarios this file's Part D rules don't cover at all (backcourt violations, defensive 3-seconds, a jump-ball redo) as the likely remaining source, rather than claiming the investigation fully closed the gap.
- Verified: computed the real correlations directly against real official NBA data and real cached play-by-play (not estimated or asserted) before writing anything down; both the module docstring (`build_stints.py`, right after the existing pbpstats-blocker note) and `STINTS_README.md`'s limitations section were updated with the exact same real numbers, kept in sync rather than only documented in one place.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the decision to actually investigate (checking substitution frequency and archetype composition specifically) rather than accept a documented-but-unexplained limitation - the user's own explicit ask, offered as an alternative to just writing it down.

## Entry 042 — new src/fit_rapm.py + src/score_lineup.py: Stage 1 feature build + Stage 2 differentially-penalized weighted ridge fit of the skill-weighted archetype-RAPM, leave-Brooklyn-out by construction (src/fit_rapm.py, src/score_lineup.py)
- Date: 2026-07-28
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): a full, detailed spec - "You are a senior sports-data ML engineer. Build src/fit_rapm.py (Stage 1 + Stage 2) and src/score_lineup.py for a skill-weighted archetype-RAPM... per-row feature vector Soff/Sdef/home_off + Eoff_1..k/Edef_1..k (skill-weighted archetype exposure) + within-side pairwise interactions, standardized, differentially-penalized ridge (talent/home unpenalized, archetype tilt + synergy penalized), GroupKFold-by-game_id CV for lambda, full-league model + a mandatory leave-one-team-out model with Brooklyn held out, a reusable score_lineup, and a validation report including join coverage and a zero-leakage assertion" - consuming build_stints.py's directed-stint table (Entry 040/041) and this project's existing recipes.csv archetype fit.
- What was used: reused this project's own `_normalize_name`/`_prefer_combined_bref_row` (step0) for the skill join rather than a third name-matching reimplementation. Skill source at this point was prior-season (2024-25) Basketball-Reference OBPM/DBPM via `load_skill()`, explicitly flagged in the file's own docstring as a default rather than assumed final (the prompt specified alpha's path explicitly but not skill's). Implemented the differential-penalty ridge as a closed-form weighted normal equation with a diagonal 0/1 penalty vector (`_fit_weighted_ridge`) rather than coercing it out of sklearn's `Ridge` (no per-column penalty argument); standardization is refit inside each CV fold on that fold's own training data only, not once up front, to avoid a real cross-validated-ridge leakage point. `build_features_single` reuses `build_features` on a synthetic one-row frame so training and scoring share identical logic - `score_lineup.py`'s `ORtg`/`DRtg`/`Net` (DRtg always vs. league-average offense, a fixed definition per the prompt's literal wording) both call it.
- Verified: `report_join_coverage` (join coverage + a `--max-drop-pct` refusal, not a silent drop) found **68.3%** of stint rows would be dropped with prior-season-only skill - investigated the cause rather than just raising the threshold, isolating that alpha-only join drops ~23% while skill-only join drops ~62%, and that all ~118 "missing skill" players are true rookies with zero 2024-25 row at all (a structural gap in "prior-season skill," not a bug or a tunable threshold). Presented this finding and paused - correctly BLOCKED at the `--max-drop-pct` refusal per its own designed behavior - rather than raising the threshold to push through; the user chose to pause and think rather than proceed, which led directly to the `build_skill.py` spec (Entry 043).
- What was NOT AI-generated: the full feature/model spec (which terms, which get penalized, LOTO construction, output schema, validation checks) - given directly and in detail. The choice of Basketball-Reference OBPM/DBPM as the default exogenous skill source (vs. waiting for an external file) was made here and explicitly flagged as a default. The decision to investigate the 68.3% drop's root cause rather than just adjust `--max-drop-pct`, and the decision to pause rather than proceed once the rookie-coverage gap was isolated, were both consistent with this session's established checkpoint discipline - the user explicitly chose "pause" when offered the option.

## Entry 043 — new src/build_skill.py fills the rookie-skill gap found in Entry 042 (draft-slot prior + replacement level, MIN-weighted veteran shrinkage); wired into fit_rapm.py, full pipeline rerun; RAPM_README.md / BUILD_SKILL_README.md (src/build_skill.py, src/fit_rapm.py, RAPM_README.md, BUILD_SKILL_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-28
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): after a clarifying "为什么skill off对于rookie是没有的呢" (why is skill_off unavailable for rookies - answered in chat, no code) and an interrupted follow-up about alternative metrics, a full spec - "Build src/build_skill.py: an EXOGENOUS skill_off/skill_def per player for 2025-26... SOURCE HIERARCHY: 1. preseason DARKO if obtainable, 2a. veterans -> prior-season DARKO/EPM, 2b. rookies -> a draft-slot prior regressed on historical rookie classes, undrafted -> replacement level... strict leakage rules (timestamp must predate 2025-26 tip-off, no in-season value under any circumstance)... validation report (coverage by source tier, a hard assertion of 0 in-season-vintage values, draft-curve shape at picks 1/15/30/55, rookie count matching the ~118 previously flagged)."
- What was used: checked DARKO (`darko.app`) and EPM (`dunksandthrees.com`) live before writing any fallback code - both only expose the current, live-updating 2025-26 season with no preseason/historical archive without a login, i.e. exactly the in-season data the prompt forbids - so every player falls through to the prompt's own pre-authorized fallback instead. Built a 471-row historical rookie-season dataset (`player_bio.DRAFT_NUMBER` x `player_advanced_bref.OBPM/DBPM`, joined on each player's TRUE rookie season - the `player_bio` row where `DRAFT_YEAR` equals that row's own season start year, not just "first season in this DB") and a 302-row historical undrafted-debut-season dataset, both **excluding the 2025-26 season from training/reference** - a real leakage trap caught by inspecting the actual joined data, not assumed clean: `player_advanced_bref` already has real, in-season 2025-26 rows for this season's own rookies (e.g. a real OBPM/DBPM row already exists for a 2025-26 first-round pick by the time this pipeline runs), so training on them would leak exactly the players the curve exists to predict; 2025-26 rookies are scored via the fitted curve's PREDICTION on their real draft number, never a lookup of their own row. Fit MIN-weighted `OBPM/DBPM ~ a + b*log(draft_number)` on 416 historical (2017-18-2024-25) rookie-seasons (weighted R²=0.102 OBPM, 0.007 DBPM - reported plainly, not hidden, that draft slot barely predicts rookie defensive impact); replacement level = MIN-weighted mean of 302 real historical undrafted debut-seasons (skill_off=-2.50, skill_def=-0.53), chosen over the unweighted mean (-4.18/-0.93) since the unweighted mean is dominated by tiny-sample noise - the same problem found next.
**A second real bug caught by inspecting real output, not assumed clean**: the veteran tier's raw distribution had OBPM ranging -19.5 to +37.9 - Alondes Williams' entire 2024-25 season was 4 minutes, and single-season BPM is a rate stat that gets extremely noisy at low minutes (20/463, 4.3%, of veteran-tier players actually in the stint table have under 50 2024-25 minutes). Fixed with standard MIN-weighted shrinkage toward the league mean (`w = MP/(MP+500)`, k=500 chosen as a round stabilization point before looking at this project's own results) - post-shrinkage range narrowed to a plausible [-4.1, +8.3], and the shrinkage target itself came out to ~0.00 for both OBPM/DBPM, consistent with BPM's known zero-mean-by-construction property (an incidental sanity check the shrinkage math is right).
Wired the resulting `players_skill.parquet` into `fit_rapm.py` as a new `load_skill_file()` (with its own independent leakage re-check, not just trusting build_skill.py's own assertion), replacing `load_skill()` as the CLI default (`--skill-season` kept only as an explicit legacy fallback for direct comparison). A `score_lineup.py` smoke test against the new holdout model initially failed with a `KeyError` on a real Brooklyn lineup - its own CLI `main()` still called the old DB-only `load_skill()` directly rather than the new file-based loader, a spot I'd missed when wiring `fit_rapm.py`; caught by actually running it end-to-end rather than assuming the fix propagated everywhere it needed to, and fixed with the same `--skill-path`/legacy-`--skill-season` pattern. Reran the full pipeline: join-coverage drop fell from 68.3% (Entry 042) to **23.1%**, and isolating it confirmed the entire remaining drop is the pre-existing >=300-min ADA archetype filter from Step 1 (0 rows now drop for missing skill) - not a new gap, so `--max-drop-pct` default raised from 0.20 to 0.25 with that reasoning stated in the CLI help text itself.
**A third thing caught by inspecting the fit, not just accepting a result**: the lambda sweep picked `lambda=1e4`, the right edge of the tested `[0.1, 1e4]` grid - a classic too-narrow-grid sign. Widened to `[0.1, 1e7]`, found the true (very shallow) interior minimum around `1.8e5`, and added a null-model/talent-only-baseline comparison (`report_variance_explained`) to explain why the sweep is so flat: null CV RMSE 114.05, talent-only 113.97, full model 113.95 - all within ~0.1%, meaning single-stint `y_off` is dominated by possession-count noise and almost nothing (talent included) explains it at that granularity, the expected textbook behavior of RAPM-style regressions (real signal shows up in the fitted coefficients once aggregated, not in per-row R²) - reported honestly rather than hidden or mistaken for a bug. The more meaningful check, lineup-level (>=100 real possessions) predicted-vs-observed ORtg for real Brooklyn lineups on the Brooklyn-holdout model, still shows r=0.95 (n=5 qualifying lineups).

**CORRECTION (see Entry 045): this r=0.95 claim was premature and is superseded.** It was ORtg-only, at n=5 - the single smallest, least-reliable sample available - and was never checked for robustness across other sample-size thresholds before being reported here as reassuring. Entry 045 found that the metric that actually matters (Net, not ORtg alone) is weak at every threshold checked (0.12-0.17 at the more meaningful n=23-50), and that the n=5/n=8 cases only looked good on ORtg due to small-sample instability. Left the original text above unedited (rather than rewritten) so the record shows what was actually claimed at the time, with this correction appended rather than silently fixed.
- Verified: ran `build_skill.py` end-to-end twice (before and after the shrinkage fix), confirming 581/581 stint-table players get a value with 0 drops both times, and that the post-shrinkage OBPM/DBPM distribution is plausible where the pre-shrinkage one wasn't. Ran `fit_rapm.py` three times (post-skill-fix with the original narrow grid, then again after widening the grid) and inspected the actual printed validation report each time rather than assuming success from a clean exit code - confirmed the join-coverage number, the lambda no longer sitting at a grid edge, and the null/talent-only/full RMSE comparison by hand-computing all three under the same GroupKFold splits before writing `report_variance_explained` into the file. Ran `score_lineup.py` as an end-to-end smoke test against the fresh `coefficients_holdout_BKN.json` for a real, high-minutes Brooklyn lineup - this is what caught the missed CLI-wiring bug above - and confirmed its output (ORtg=117.58) matches that same lineup's row in `fit_rapm.py`'s own holdout sanity-check table exactly, i.e. both entry points are computing identically from the same saved model.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the full build_skill.py spec (source hierarchy, exogeneity rule, output schema, validation report contents) - given directly and in detail. The decision to add MIN-weighted shrinkage for the veteran tier, and to widen the lambda grid + add the variance-explained baseline report, were both judgment calls made here after finding the underlying data/fit issues during verification - flagged as refinements/bug-catches consistent with CLAUDE.md's allowance for those (not changes to the core research question or method), not run past the user first given the "Auto Mode" instruction to make the reasonable call and let the user redirect if needed.

## Entry 044 — investigated (then reverted) a Step 1 archetype-basis refit: fixes a real name-join miss (Ronald Holland II) but reshuffles 4 of 8 archetype exemplars, making hand-written labels stale (data/basis_2025_26/, RAPM_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-28
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): the user asked "why are there still 23.1% rows dropped" after Entry 043's fix. Explained the 148 alpha-missing players are (almost all) the intended >=300-min ADA filter, but investigating further (checking each missing player's real MIN rather than assuming the filter fully explains it) surfaced a genuine anomaly: Ronald Holland II has 1,550 real minutes yet has no archetype recipe at all. Asked whether to fix it; user said yes.
- What was used: traced the anomaly to `step0_data_collect_process.py`'s B-Ref name join - Basketball-Reference lists him as "Ron Holland," NBA.com as "Ronald Holland II," and confirmed live that `build_nba_side_tables()` already resolves this correctly via an existing `_NAME_ALIASES` entry (itself added in an earlier session specifically for this player, per its own code comment) - meaning `data/basis_2025_26/recipes.csv` on disk simply predates that fix (file mtimes confirmed: `step0_data_collect_process.py` was last modified AFTER `recipes.csv`). Backed up the existing basis directory (`data/basis_2025_26_pre_holland_fix_backup/` - a reversible safety copy, not a destructive in-place edit) and reran `step1_archetypes_model.phase2_build(k=8, min_threshold=300, season_min="2025-26", season_max="2025-26", seeds=(0,1,2))` to regenerate.
- **Found before declaring success, not after**: the refit did correctly add Ronald Holland II (434 rows vs. 433), but comparing the OLD and NEW `archetype_definitions.csv` by hand showed 4 of the 8 archetype exemplars changed to a DIFFERENT real player (Tim Hardaway Jr.->Klay Thompson, Shai Gilgeous-Alexander->Luka Dončić, Bez Mbeng->Bryce McGowens, D'Angelo Russell->none) - ADA picks real players sitting on the population's convex hull as exemplars, and one added row shifted the z-scoring enough to flip which players sit there for nearly half the archetypes. Ran `phase3_diagnose` on the new fit and found this breaks `step1_archetypes_model.py`'s own hand-written `ARCHETYPE_TO_PAPER` dict (its own docstring: "re-review it by hand if the basis is ever refit and the archetype indices/exemplars shift") - e.g. archetype 1's exemplar was now Dončić but the note text still described Valanciunas' stat profile, unchanged since it's keyed by index not re-derived from the live fit. Also flagged that `STINTS_README.md`'s own documented finding ("arch_7 = Mbeng-type, r~+0.63 with the pace gap") now refers to a different real exemplar (McGowens) under the new fit.
- Presented this full tradeoff to the user (small fix, real relabeling+re-verification cost) rather than silently completing what had been framed as a small join fix, since the actual scope (re-reviewing 4 archetype labels, re-checking already-built downstream content for stale player references) turned out much larger than implied when first asking permission. Recommended reverting given one bench player (~0.7% of total possessions, per Entry 043's own possession-weighted check) didn't justify that cost. Recomputed GATE 1 using the spec's OWN metric (possession-weighted player-APPEARANCE non-join rate, not the row-level "23.1% of rows dropped" framing already in use) - a materially different, more lenient number (3.11%, comfortably under the 5% threshold) since a row with 1-of-10 players missing only loses 1/10 of its possession-weighted appearance mass, not the whole row. Added `feature_min`/`feature_max` (observed standardized-range envelope from the final training refit) to `save_model()`'s output and a standalone `data/model/league_average_opponent.json`, intended to back a planned extrapolation flag in `score_lineup.py` - the flag itself was never implemented once GATE 2 stopped Phase 3 (see below), so these fields currently sit unused, left in place rather than reverted since they're harmless.
**Formalized GATE 2** by extending `sanity_check_holdout` to compute OBSERVED NET (not just ORtg) - aggregating BOTH sides of the stint table for each real Brooklyn 5-man unit (offense-side rows for observed ORtg, defense-side rows for observed DRtg) and comparing against the holdout model's PREDICTED Net (`score_lineup`'s own Net field). Caught a real pandas bug immediately on first run: `.loc[tuple_lineup, "column"]` raised `AssertionError`/"Too many indexers" - a DataFrame index containing raw Python tuples (from grouping by a tuple-valued column) makes `.loc[]` with a (tuple, column) pair ambiguous, since pandas tries to unpack the tuple itself as an additional indexer; fixed by switching to `.at[tuple_lineup, "column"]`, verified directly on a minimal reproduction before trusting the fix on the real data.
**Real finding**: at the spec's own `min_poss=100`, Net correlation = 0.116, below the spec's own 0.3 threshold - a genuine GATE 2 failure. Rather than accept or dismiss this at face value, checked robustness across `min_poss` in {30,50,75,100} (n=50/23/8/5 lineups) before reporting anything: Net correlation stayed weak at every threshold (0.13/0.17/0.50/0.12), with MAE 19-22 points/100 possessions at the two more statistically meaningful thresholds (n=23,50) - larger than the entire realistic range of NBA net ratings. This directly exposed that Entry 043's earlier "r=0.95 against real Brooklyn lineups" claim (made two entries ago in this same log) was ORtg-only, computed at n=5 (the single smallest, least-reliable cut available), and had never been checked for robustness before being reported as reassuring - corrected in place in Entry 043 (appended a correction note, left the original claim visible rather than editing it away) rather than quietly fixed. Diagnosed three concrete likely causes rather than stopping at "it failed": (1) heavy ridge shrinkage (already documented in Entry 043) leaves predicted Net almost flat across real lineups (~3.7-point total spread at n=5), mechanically capping any achievable correlation; (2) lineup-level defensive rating is intrinsically noisy at 100-500 possessions; (3) observed Net reflects whatever real opponents Brooklyn actually faced (variable, unadjusted), while predicted Net assumes a fixed league-average opponent - a real definitional mismatch, not just sample noise.
Presented the full finding (not a summary) plus 4 concrete options via AskUserQuestion; user chose to stop and treat it as an honest negative finding rather than proceed to Phase 3/4 (roster-deployment lineup enumeration/scoring, archetype marginal-value "needs" ranking) on an unvalidated model, redirecting future Roster Construction work to `step4_roster_construction.py`'s already-validated WLS mixture model instead. ## Entry 047 — six-angle investigation into GATE 2's failure (Entry 045), each proposed by the user as a specific, testable hypothesis; a real multi-season data-pipeline bug found and fixed along the way; converged on a documented negative result rather than a fix (src/build_stints.py, src/build_skill.py, data/rc_multiseason/, RAPM_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-29
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized): a long, interactive debugging session in which the user proposed one specific, falsifiable hypothesis at a time for why GATE 2 failed, asked me to test each directly against real data rather than reason about it abstractly, and used the results to decide the next hypothesis. In order: (1) "why correlation not R²/variance" - computed proper out-of-sample R²; (2) "try many regression formulas, see if any works" - simplified to an 8-feature skill-weighted-difference model; (3) "why not a tree model/LightGBM" - reasoned through (not run) why this likely wouldn't help and would sacrifice interpretability; (4) "stints are too short (~3 possessions avg) - set a minimum-length threshold" - swept possession-count filters; (5) "split the possessions of a real lineup in half and correlate the two halves - that's the empirical reliability ceiling" - the single most decisive test; (6) "drop the skill axis, archetype distribution only"; (7) "extend to 3 seasons of data"; (8) "aggregate to the team-season level, compare against real official Net rating - 30 teams x 3 seasons = 90 reliable points."
- What was used/found, per hypothesis:
  1. Real out-of-sample R² on the pooled 1317-lineup sample: R²≈0.04 (Net), R²≈0.01 (ORtg) - worse than the correlation numbers implied. Predicted Net's own std (5.4) vs. observed Net's std (28.9) directly explained why: the model barely varies its answer across different real lineups.
  2. 8-feature simplification (`Eoff_a - Edef_a`, no interactions/talent/home) evaluated on the same pooled 30-team task: corr=0.131, WORSE than the original 75-feature model's 0.212 - ruled out "too many interaction terms" as the cause.
  3. Reasoned through why ridge over a tree model/LightGBM was the right choice given the diagnosis (low signal-to-noise, not nonlinearity ridge can't express; interpretability is a real project requirement, not just a preference) - not implemented, since the diagnosis pointed away from model-class as the fix.
  4. Possession-count floor swept at 1/3/5/8/12: pooled Net corr stayed flat (0.20-0.22) across the entire range, becoming uncomputable at 16 (833 training rows). Possession-weighting already handles this noise in the loss function; filtering only discarded data for no measurable gain.
  5. **Split-half reliability test** (the most decisive result in this entry): for every real 2025-26 lineup, independently randomly split its own directed stint rows into two halves (one random draw per row, not per lineup, so a single real stint's two reciprocal directed rows could land in different halves - confirmed this doesn't bias the estimate, since each half's ORtg and DRtg for a given lineup are independently-sampled subsets of that lineup's own offense-role and defense-role data respectively, not required to come from the same underlying stint), computed observed Net for each half, correlated the two halves against each other. Result: **-0.10 to -0.43 depending on the minimum-possessions-per-half threshold** (n=705/262/138) - the empirical reliability ceiling for "this specific lineup's Net rating in one season" is AT OR BELOW ZERO, meaning the target itself isn't a stably measurable quantity at this data volume, independent of any model or feature choice.
  6. Refit with `skill_off=skill_def=1.0` for every player (making `Eoff_a` a pure archetype-share sum; `Soff`/`Sdef` dropped since they become a structural constant - initially caused a `LinAlgError: Singular matrix` from a zero-variance unpenalized column, fixed by dropping those 2 columns and their penalty entries before fitting): corr=0.199, R²=0.037 - statistically indistinguishable from the skill-weighted version.
  7. Pulled 2023-24 and 2024-25 via `build_stints.py`. **Found and fixed a real bug**: `combine_and_validate()` globbed the ENTIRE shared `data/stints/games/` cache directory (which holds every game ever pulled, across all seasons, since per-game files are just `{game_id}.parquet` with no season subdirectory) instead of filtering to the requested season's own game_ids - caught because the first 2023-24 combine reported a physically impossible ~190 possessions/game (2x real pace) and "2465 games checked" (~2x the real 1230) for score reconciliation. Root-caused by checking `stints_2023_24.parquet`'s actual game_id composition directly (found 1230 real `00223` games AND 1230 `00225`/2025-26 games mixed together) rather than trusting the printed report. **Immediately checked whether `stints_2025_26.parquet` itself - the file every prior GATE 2/split-half/formula-testing result in this whole investigation was built on - was also contaminated**: confirmed clean (1230/1230, all `00225`), because it happened to be combined before any other season's games existed in the shared directory, not because the bug didn't apply to it. `stints_2024_25.parquet` WAS contaminated the same way (1230 of its 1235 "games" were actually 2025-26 duplicates; only 266 real 2024-25 games had ever actually been pulled, despite an earlier validation report claiming 1235 parsed successfully). Fixed `combine_and_validate()` to filter `files` by `games_df["GAME_ID"]` before combining anything; regenerated both affected seasons and reverified their game_id composition directly post-fix, not just re-trusted the new printed report.
  8. Extended archetype coverage to all 3 seasons via `step1_archetypes_model.py`'s existing `project()` function - projecting 2023-24/2024-25 players onto the ALREADY-FITTED, ALREADY-LABELED 2025-26 basis (never refitting), specifically to avoid repeating the archetype-exemplar-reshuffle problem from the earlier Ronald Holland investigation (Entry 044) - saved to a NEW `data/rc_multiseason/recipes_multiseason.csv`, `data/basis_2025_26/recipes.csv` (the portal's own file) untouched. Extended skill coverage by re-running `build_skill.py`'s already-parameterized `build_skill_table(stint_season, skill_season, ...)` for 2023-24 (skill_season=2022-23) and 2024-25 (skill_season=2023-24) - no code changes needed, confirming the file's existing design already generalized correctly. **Team-season-level test** (the user's own proposed angle, and the most decisive of the whole investigation): for each of 30 teams x 3 seasons, scored that team's ENTIRE season of real stints (both sides) with its own leave-one-team-out holdout model, possession-weighted the predictions up to one team-season Net number, and compared against that team's REAL OFFICIAL `NET_RATING` (`LeagueDashTeamStats` - an independent ground truth, not derived from this project's own stint table). Full model pooled corr=0.770 (n=90) vs. a talent-only baseline (same model, archetype/synergy coefficients zeroed) at corr=0.767 - statistically indistinguishable, and talent-only actually WON in 2 of the 3 individual seasons.
- Verified: every number in this entry was computed directly against real data at the time it was reported (no estimates or extrapolations) - including re-deriving the exact single-stint-row-level predicted-vs-actual correlation (0.0345) on request mid-investigation to concretely confirm the user's own restated understanding of why the model fails, and independently re-confirming `stints_2025_26.parquet`'s game_id composition before continuing to rely on any of its prior results once the combine bug was found.

Documented, rather than hid, that CBBD's shot-TYPE categories can't split into the NBA model's separate 3-10/10-16/16-3P buckets at population scale - both members of each pair get the same collapsed population z-score, not a fabricated split. Height/Dist./Corner-3% used the NBA training population's own scale as the most defensible available fallback (height because it's league-invariant; the other two because no real NCAA population baseline was obtainable within scope) - all three flagged explicitly as the weakest-support features in the vector, not silently treated as equally reliable.
  - **A real bug caught by an absurd result, not assumed correct from a clean-looking number**: the first z-scored vector showed Corner 3s_%3PA at z=130.88 - traced to comparing Brown's own percentage-scale computation (23.23) against the NBA training population's fraction-scale statistics (mu=0.28) - `player_shooting_bref` stores this column as a 0-1 fraction, verified directly against a real row (Luka Dončić's actual Corner 3s_%3PA = 0.058, not 5.8). Root-caused to a SEPARATE bug in the verification step itself: an earlier manual scale-check had queried `player_advanced_bref` (which doesn't have these columns) instead of `player_shooting_bref` - SQLite silently returned the literal column-name strings instead of raising a clear error, and this wasn't caught by eyeballing the tuple output at the time. Re-verified against the correct table before trusting anything downstream. Confirmed the OTHER 6 shot-location z-scores were NOT affected (both sides of each of those specific comparisons used the same CBBD-percentage scale internally, genuinely consistent) - fixed only the one affected line rather than blanket-guessing every feature needed the same /100 correction.
  Solved the simplex-constrained projection (`min ||x-a·Z|| s.t. a>=0, sum(a)=1`) directly in the already-standardized z-space (bypassing `project()`'s own internal re-standardization, which assumes RAW NBA-scale input) - the correct way to combine an already-cross-population-normalized vector with a basis expressed in its own z-space. Result: 59.1% Luka Dončić-type, sanity-checked against his real public scouting profile (shot-creating lead guard, not a big) before treating it as trustworthy.
  - **Phase B2**: projected 2023-24 and 2024-25 NBA populations onto the SAME reduced basis (reusing `project()`, not the original 29-feature basis, for a genuinely apples-to-apples comparable pool) - 1,277 total player-seasons. Computed cosine similarity AND Euclidean distance (both, as explicitly requested for this specific deliverable - noted this differs from this project's own established Jensen-Shannon-distance convention used elsewhere, used cosine/Euclidean here as literally instructed rather than substituting the project's usual metric unprompted). Found and fixed a real duplicate-row bug in the rookie-season comp list (player_bio has one row per season per player; an un-deduplicated merge fanned out matching rows) - deduplicated before reporting, resulting in 7 genuine unique comps rather than a padded, partially-duplicated 10.
  - **Phase B3**: reused this project's OWN existing, already-validated similarity-weighted pooling machinery (`compute_style_pool_by_vector` + `similarity_weighted_benchmark`, built earlier in this session for the Brown slot-query portal feature) directly on Brown's real projected vector - not a new implementation. This gives a materially larger, more stable pooled sample (n=434, ESS=104.5 unconditional; n=207, ESS=50.2 for the positive-net-rating subset) than a fixed top-10-comp average would. Explicitly labeled the output evidence synthesis, not causal inference, per the prompt's own requirement.
  - **Phase B4**: checked each archetype's REAL fitted profile (`describe_archetypes`) before writing any constraint-flag logic, rather than assuming what each archetype represents from its exemplar name alone - this directly overturned a prior assumption: archetype 3 ("Kam Jones"-exemplar) turned out to be specifically a LOW-EFFICIENCY ball-handler type (high TOV%/AST%, but low TS%/BPM/FTr), while archetype 6 (Brown's own dominant type, "Dončić"-exemplar) is a HIGH-efficiency creator - and Phase B3's own pooled evidence showed pairing Brown with MORE of archetype 6 is the single most POSITIVE association, while archetype 3 is the single most NEGATIVE one. Built the "second-guard redundancy" flag and incompatibility list around this specific, verified distinction (inefficient ball-handling, not "any second guard") rather than the generic assumption the task prompt itself suggested ("expect this to name guards") - the flag still names guards (Saraf/Traoré/Powell/Mann), but for a more specific, falsifiable reason than "he's a guard." Used `build_skill.py`'s already-validated draft-slot-prior curve directly (pick 6 -> skill_off=-1.42, skill_def=-0.57) for Brown's own talent number, and `players_skill.parquet`'s real exogenous skill values (NOT the RAPM/archetype-synergy model, which Task A's investigation found fails its own out-of-sample validation gate) for the 16 real-recipe Nets players - the explicit talent-vs-archetype design boundary from Task A, applied here rather than just stated. Enumerated all C(16,4)=1,820 real complements; reported near-misses with their specific violated flags rather than only the passing set.
  - **Phase B5**: cross-referenced Phase B3's ranked success-pattern against Phase B4's actual roster-supply counts per archetype - found the roster is heaviest on the SMALLEST positive-association archetype (shooting, +0.3pp, 7 players) and completely missing the THIRD-largest positive one (perimeter defense/Thybulle-type, +0.6pp, 0 players), while the single largest positive archetype (co-star creation, +1.6pp) is already real-supplied (Randle, Porter Jr.) - not treated as a gap. No target board built (no candidate-pool file was provided, per the prompt's own explicit "skip unless provided" instruction).
- Verified: every number in `docs/BROWN_STUDY.md` and `data/brown/*.json` traces to a real API response, a real cross-check against an independent source (his real season FGA/3PA totals, a live re-pull of `player_shooting_bref`'s real Luka Dončić row), or a real computation over real bulk-pulled population data (n=3,114 for box-score features, n=2,810 for shot-type features) - none fabricated or estimated. Renamed the 9 working scripts from an underscore-prefixed scratch convention (`_brown_*.py`) to the project's normal convention (`brown_*.py`) once the pipeline was confirmed working end-to-end, since they're real, reusable, reproducible pipeline code, not throwaway exploration.

## Entry 054 — rebuilt Diagnostic Analysis's per-player card into a six-section report (Sections A-F: purity/entropy, role drift + development comps, role elasticity, miscasting test, signature radar, stability bootstrap) — new src/step2b_player_diagnostics.py, extensive src/portal.py additions, new DIAGNOSTICS_README.md
- Date: 2026-07-29

- Prompt (summarized): a full spec, given as "a senior sports-data engineer," for rebuilding the Diagnostic Analysis page's per-player card into sections A-F, in response to my own earlier finding (prompted by "针对 Diagnostic Analysis 我觉得研究的是不是太浅了一点") that the page's benchmark used an unconditional "all similar players" baseline rather than a success-filtered one, and a side-by-side read of the "Scouting Anyone" paper's own Section 6.1 case study. The spec required: reusing every existing validated function verbatim (macro_archetype_exposure, similarity_weighted_benchmark, mismatch_score, teammate_lift, screening_table, load_player_pairs, project(), recipes_frame(), render_profile_card, archetype_column_chart, diverging_bar, render_player_stats_tab, _build_sortable_table_html) with no second implementation of any of them; explicit gates (0a/0b/C3/F) that must fail loudly and report a downgrade rather than fake finer-grained data; every claim labeled descriptive, never a win predictor.
- What was used: **Phase 0** — `build_historical_recipes()` projects 2023-24/2024-25 onto the FIXED 2025-26 basis (never a refit) via `project()`/`recipes_frame()` unchanged; Gate 0a verified zero missing features across all 29 basis columns for both historical seasons (checked directly, not assumed from the multi-season RAPM work having pulled similar data); Gate 0b printed 10 stable veterans' 3-season recipes and confirmed smooth evolution (Jokić, SGA, Gobert, Bam, Brunson, etc. - no wild jumps), catching the exact mu/sd-recomputed-per-season bug this gate exists to catch. **Section A** extends the existing card with purity (`max(alpha)`) and normalized entropy, each with a real league percentile and tercile label computed from the actual 2025-26 population distribution. **Section B1** role drift: major-archetype line chart (any archetype exceeding 10% share in any season; the rest pooled into one "Minor" line) with an automatic riser/faller caption; correctly reports "insufficient" (not padded) for a player like E.J. Liddell whose only qualifying season is 2025-26. **Section B2** development comps: age-matched (±1yr) JS-similarity search across all 3 projected seasons, then reports each comp's recipe the FOLLOWING season in our window, with the lookahead-length caveat stated on-page since 3 seasons caps it at 2. **Section C1/C2** are the existing mismatch/teammate-lift blocks, moved unchanged, with the existing B/C-agreement verdict. **Section C3** role elasticity: after determining that true per-event individual usage attribution would require re-deriving stint-to-PBP event boundaries (build_stints.py's own substitution-tracking logic, not exposed as reusable output) - judged out of proportion for one of six sections - implemented the sanctioned downgrade instead: a TEAM-level shot-profile split (eFG%/3PA-rate/TOV%) using the already-validated stints_2025_26_v2.parquet process-metric columns, comparing stints with vs. without his highest-shared-minutes teammate on the floor, explicitly labeled "team-level proxy, not an individual usage split" everywhere it appears. **Section D** miscasting test: split the basis's 29 features into 19 opportunity (usage, shot-location mix, assisted-share, all 9 playtype shares) vs. 9 outcome (TS%/AST%/TOV%/STL%/BLK%/TRB%/FTr/BPM) features, with height and average shot distance's bucket assignment as disclosed judgment calls (documented in DIAGNOSTICS_README.md, not hidden); partial projections built by setting the OTHER block's raw feature values to the basis's own population mean (zeros the z-score) and reusing `project()` unmodified - refactored `miscasting_score`/`archetype_centroids`/`player_signature` to accept a pre-loaded population frame so the league-wide percentile (433 players) computes in ~1.2s instead of reloading the full population per player. **Section E1** signature radar (new `go.Scatterpolar` chart type, not previously in the portal): centroid = real mean z-vector of every player whose own top archetype matches his, axes = top-10-loading features plus any feature with |deviation|>1SD, top-3 deviations listed in plain language. **Section E2** confirmed genuinely out of scope (no salary table anywhere in the DB or repo - checked directly) and skipped with a one-line note per the spec's own instruction not to scrape mid-task. **Section F** bootstrap: after determining full per-game USG%/AST%/TRB%/STL%/BLK%/BPM recomputation needs team+opponent context not worth building for one subsection, downgraded further (disclosed) to bootstrapping only the 4 features with individual-only formulas (TS%, FTr, TOV%, an approximate PTS_PER_100), holding the other 25 fixed - games discovered via the already-loaded stint table's own player-roster columns (handles a traded player's two-team season for free), read from data/stints/raw_cache/*_box.parquet, B=500 in <1s per player, cached per (player_id, season, B) behind a "Run stability bootstrap" button so it never reruns on an unrelated rerun.
- Verified: every step2b_player_diagnostics.py function tested directly against real data before wiring to the UI - Gate 0a/0b outputs printed and read; Section A/B/D/E numbers computed for Michael Porter Jr. and sanity-checked (e.g. his miscast score's "underused" archetype and top signature deviations both matched his real public scouting profile); role_drift's "insufficient" path verified on a real rookie (Egor Dëmin) and development_comps verified on the same rookie (real, plausible comps: Bub Carrington, Nick Smith Jr.); role_elasticity and bootstrap_recipe verified on a traded player (Julius Randle) to confirm cross-team game discovery works. Then launched a real Streamlit instance and drove it with Playwright end to end: confirmed no exception/traceback anywhere on the page across two different players (default-selected E.J. Liddell and a second player selected via a real chart click, Day'Ron Sharpe), screenshotted every section (A through F, the B/C agreement box, the updated Limitations block), and clicked the live "Run stability bootstrap" button to confirm Section F's chart actually renders with real error bars in the browser, not just in a standalone script.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the six-section structure, every formula (purity/entropy, the exact opportunity/outcome feature split, the bootstrap's 4-vs-25 feature split, the age-matching and lookahead framing for development comps), and every gate/downgrade requirement (0a, 0b, C3, F) - all specified directly in the task prompt. The specific judgment calls flagged as such (which features count as "neutral" in Section D, exactly which 4 features are individually-derivable in Section F) were made here but disclosed on-page and in DIAGNOSTICS_README.md rather than presented as settled by the spec.

## Entry 058 — four layout/display fixes across Sections B, C3, and E, plus a real bug found and fixed during live verification of C3 (src/portal.py, src/step2b_player_diagnostics.py)
- Date: 2026-07-29
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized, four separate mid-turn messages): (1) "B1. Role drift across seasons 这个图的X轴长度太长了 稍微窄一点" (B1's chart x-axis is too long, narrow it); (2) "B2里的表格使用和上面的table一样的格式 不要看起来觉得不一致" (B2's table should match the format of the table above - don't let it look inconsistent); (3) "C3...展示的eFG% 3PA rate TOV% 我觉得首先可以挑选更多指标 其次是这个显示效果不好 或者说很差 可以用别的方式么" (C3's metrics - pick more of them, and the display is poor, use a different form); (4) "E...把图像放在左侧 文字和说明放在右侧 并且图的字体太小了" (E - chart on the left, text on the right, chart font too small).
- What was used: **(1)** wrapped B1's Plotly line chart in `st.columns([2, 1])` (chart in the wider column) instead of `width="stretch"`, so 2-3 season categories don't stretch across the full container. **(2)** added the missing leftmost Photo column (`HEADSHOT_URL`, circular avatar) + bold player name to B2's `dev_comps_table`, matching `render_screening_table`'s exact cell pattern - the one sortable table in the file missing it. **(3)** extended `role_elasticity()`'s `_summarize()` in step2b_player_diagnostics.py with 3 more metrics from the SAME already-validated stint columns (no new gate) - ORtg (points per 100 poss.), FG%, 3P% - alongside the existing eFG%/3PA rate/TOV% (6 total); replaced the 3-column `st.metric` block with a new `_with_without_row()` paired-bar component (with-teammate=dark ink bar, without-teammate=light grey bar, each row normalized against its own max so percentage-scale and count-scale metrics like ORtg can share one component without a misleading shared axis). **(4)** bumped `signature_radar_chart`'s angular/radial/legend font sizes (10→13-14) and switched `render_section_e` to `st.columns([7, 5])` (radar left, deviation callouts + E2 note right) instead of a single stacked block.
- **A real bug found and fixed during live verification, not asked for**: item (3)'s first version passed the concatenated multi-row HTML through `st.markdown(unsafe_allow_html=True)` - live-screenshotting it showed the FIRST row rendered as a real bar but every row after it rendered as literal HTML source text. Root-caused (not just patched): `st.markdown` still runs content through a CommonMark parser before honoring `unsafe_allow_html`, and CommonMark treats a line indented ≥4 spaces as a literal code block - concatenating several multi-line, indented f-string blocks reproduces that pattern at each block boundary. Fixed by switching to `components.html()` (the same reason every sortable table in this file already uses it instead of `st.markdown` for multi-row HTML) rather than hand-stripping indentation, which would be fragile against the next edit. Also caught and fixed an iframe-height underestimate (the last row was clipped) by measuring the real rendered row height (80px, not the initially-guessed 62px) against a live screenshot before finalizing.

Role drift across seasons 颜色重复了" (the colors repeat) - BL_GREEN and BL_GREEN_SOFT read as the same color in a thin line + small legend swatch; (2) "screening scatter plot里选中球员不要用绿色圆圈 可以用别的方式 并且在screning table里点击球员头像也可以更新球员 不是光从scatter plot里更新" (don't use a green selection ring - some other way is fine - and clicking a photo in the screening table should also update the selected player, not only the scatter plot); (3) a full spec for rewriting Section A's Purity/Entropy/Stability copy (shared explanatory caption, direction-free percentile language replacing "X% percentile - label", both progress bars encoding percentile with a legend and a league-median tick, and a computed tight/moderate/wide stability verdict with stated thresholds); (4) a live question ("But it means that bootstrap doesn't work right?") after the user saw the bootstrap's own honesty caption and worried it signaled a bug - answered directly (it's the intended disclosure, not an error; the bootstrap ran correctly).
- What was used: **(1)** loaded the project's dataviz skill, replaced the role-drift line chart's ad hoc 8-color list with the skill's own validated reference categorical palette (blue/orange/aqua/yellow/magenta/green/violet/red), re-validated against this app's real BL_PAPER surface with `validate_palette.py` before adopting it (all hard gates pass; 4 slots warn on contrast, mitigated by the chart's existing legend+hover per the skill's "relief rule") rather than hand-picking replacement colors and eyeballing them. **(2a)** swapped the quadrant chart's selection-ring color from BL_GREEN to BL_GOLD (BKN's own jersey color was blending into photos of players wearing green jerseys) and thickened it slightly (3px→4px) for visibility. **(2b)** attempted photo-click-to-select on the screening table: first tried a relative `<a href="?diag_pid=X" target="_top">` and caught a bug before shipping it (components.html() renders via `srcdoc`, whose base URL is `about:srcdoc`, not the parent page - a relative href would resolve wrong); switched to `onclick="window.top.location.search=...".` Live-tested this against a real click and found it silently did nothing; root-caused with a direct `evaluate()` call inside the iframe, which surfaced the real blocker: `SecurityError: ...does not have permission to navigate the target frame` - Streamlit's `components.html()` iframe sandbox (`allow-forms allow-modals allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox allow-same-origin allow-scripts allow-downloads`) is missing `allow-top-navigation`/`allow-top-navigation-by-user-activation`, and without either flag the browser hard-blocks ALL top-frame navigation from inside that iframe regardless of technique (JS assignment or a real link click) - a platform constraint with no public Streamlit API to work around from this file. Reverted the table photo back to non-clickable rather than ship a silently-broken feature, and left the (still correct, still tested) `st.query_params` READ side in `render_diagnostic_analysis` in place since it's real, working infrastructure a future native-widget alternative (e.g. `st.dataframe`'s own `on_select` row-selection) could still use. **(3)** rewrote `_stat_gauge_card()`: fixed a real second bug found while implementing this (the tick mark was set to `pct*100` - the exact same value as the fill bar's own width - so it was redundantly marking his own percentile a second time instead of the league median, which in percentile-space is always exactly 50); added a shared caption above both cards; built `_specialization_sentence()` with the two metrics' opposite percentile-inversion rules worked out algebraically and cross-checked against a real prior example (E.J. Liddell: pu_pct=13%→"more hybrid than 87%"; en_pct=94%→"more hybrid than 94%" - purity needs `1-pct`, entropy doesn't, because the two `tercile_label()` calls' low/mid/high argument order is intentionally mirrored); rewrote the stability caption with a computed tight(≤3pp)/moderate(≤8pp)/wide(>8pp) verdict word, thresholds stated in DIAGNOSTICS_README.md as requested. **(4)** answered the bootstrap-caption question directly - confirmed via the real numbers already in the caption (79 games, B=500) that the bootstrap had in fact run successfully, and explained the caption is a permanent, by-design disclosure that appears every time the bootstrap succeeds, not an error state.
- Verified: launched a fresh Streamlit instance on a spare port for each round of changes, drove it with Playwright, selected Julius Randle via a real chart click each time, and confirmed no exception at every step. Screenshotted the role-drift chart (5 distinct, non-duplicate line colors), the quadrant chart (gold ring, clearly visible against a green jersey photo), and Section A's rewritten cards pre- and post-bootstrap (shared caption present, median tick now centered independent of fill width, both sentences reading correctly, and the computed "tight band" verdict firing correctly at a real 1pp resampled range for Randle's 29% Inside Scoring Big share). The table-click SecurityError was reproduced and confirmed via a direct in-iframe `evaluate()` call, not inferred - a real, verified root cause before deciding to revert rather than continuing to iterate on non-functional variants.
- What was NOT AI-generated: all four requests and their exact requirements (which colors were duplicating, why the ring was hard to see, the click-to-select requirement, and every numbered item in the copy-rewrite spec including the example wording and the threshold values) - given directly. The srcdoc base-URL bug, the sandbox-permission root cause, the tick-mark bug, and the decision to revert rather than ship a broken feature were all found and made here, not asked for.

## Entry 065 — Section B's signature radar replaced: archetype-membership centroid → similarity-weighted "style neighbors" centroid (src/step2b_player_diagnostics.py, src/portal.py, DIAGNOSTICS_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-29
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (summarized, a two-step exchange): first "但是这个图里我有个疑问 比如Danny Wolf 其实他只有28%的Combo Guard 他是一个持球型的大个子 但是你画图的话就会导致他的特征其实离传统的Combo Guard非常远其实 你怎么想这个问题呢" (a question about Danny Wolf: he's only 28% Combo Guard, a ball-handling big, but comparing him to the traditional Combo Guard centroid would make his features look very far from it - what do I think?), then, after I confirmed the concern was real with actual numbers and proposed a fix, "我觉得这个radar chart不应该是针对单一的archetype 而是针对比如和他风格最相近的比如10个人 他们在features上的差距 你觉得这个猜想合理么" (this radar chart shouldn't compare against a single archetype - it should compare against the ~10 players whose style is most similar to him - is this reasonable?), confirmed with "yes please."
- What was used: verified the failure case with real data before agreeing it was real - Danny Wolf: Combo Guard 27.9% / 3&D Wing 23.6% / Rim Protector 20.5% (a near three-way tie), purity percentile 4.8% (more hybrid than 95% of the league) - confirming the old archetype-membership bucket would compare him against players who are mostly much purer guards than he is. Replaced `archetype_centroids()` (mean z-vector of everyone whose TOP archetype matches, a hard membership bucket) with `similarity_weighted_feature_centroid()` - reused the EXACT weighting convention `similarity_weighted_benchmark()` (`step2_diagnostic_analysis.py`) already uses and this project already validated (Jensen-Shannon distance over the player's full recipe vector, weight=(1-JS)^power, power=4.0), applied to raw z-scored features instead of teammate archetype exposure, rather than building a second, different "similarity" definition or literally averaging a hard top-10 nearest-neighbor cutoff (which would have far higher variance at n≈10 - the new approach's effective sample size is reported on-page, e.g. ESS≈181 for a real player, vs. what a literal top-10 average would give). Updated `player_signature()` to accept a pre-computed `centroid` vector directly (dropped the `top_archetype`/`centroids`-array indexing it no longer needs) and updated all three call sites (`render_section_e`/Section B, the paused PDF export, `render_scouting_summary`) plus the cached wrapper (`load_centroids` → `load_peer_centroid`) consistently. Reworded the on-page copy that referenced "the {archetype} centroid" (the section header, the radar legend, and the Scouting Summary sentence) to "style neighbors" instead, since the comparison group is no longer named by a single archetype label; added an on-page caption stating the new methodology and its ESS so a thin peer group is never silently treated as solid.
- Verified: ran `similarity_weighted_feature_centroid()` directly against Danny Wolf's real data before touching the UI (ESS≈181, ordering of top deviations changed sensibly to PLAYER_HEIGHT_INCHES/PLAYTYPE_HANDOFF - both genuinely distinctive for a ball-handling big compared to *other* ball-handling-type players, not just "different from pure guards"). Then launched a fresh Streamlit instance, drove it with Playwright, confirmed no exception, and screenshotted both Section B (new caption + ESS + "style neighbors" framing rendering correctly) and the Scouting Summary box (its own sentence updated to match, no longer claiming "that archetype's typical profile").
- What was NOT AI-generated: the core idea (compare against style-neighbors instead of a single archetype bucket) and catching the Danny Wolf case that motivated it - both the user's own, developed across a back-and-forth where I confirmed the concern was real before agreeing a change was warranted. The choice to reuse the existing similarity-weighted-benchmark convention (rather than a literal top-10-nearest-neighbors average) was made here as the correct way to implement the user's idea, not asked for verbatim.

## Entry 066 — Section B's radar: plain-language feature names, category-grouped axes, and better/worse color-coding on the deviation bullets (src/portal.py)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code

- What was used: added `FEATURE_LABELS` (raw code → short plain name, e.g. "PLAYTYPE_PRBALLHANDLER" → "P&R ball-handler") and `FEATURE_CATEGORY`/`CATEGORY_ORDER` (5 groups: Box score, Physical, Shot profile, Shot creation, Play type) - both reused for the radar axis labels AND the deviation bullets so the wording matches in both places. `axis_idx` is now stable-sorted by category before building the chart. For color-coding, reused step2b's OWN existing `OUTCOME_FEATURES` split (built earlier for Section E's miscasting test) rather than inventing a second "good/bad" classification - only those 9 stats get a green/coral judgment (with `HIGHER_IS_BETTER` flagging TOV% as the one where lower is better); every deployment/style feature (play type, shot location, height) stays neutral ink, since "he does more hand-offs than his peers" isn't good or bad, just different - a distinction stated explicitly in a caption, not left for the reader to guess.

- Verified: launched a fresh Streamlit instance, drove it with Playwright, confirmed no exception, and screenshotted Section B - plain-language axis labels and bullet text render correctly, the "Steal rate" bullet (a real outcome feature, below his style neighbors' typical) renders in coral while the two stylistic bullets stay neutral ink, and the axes form contiguous same-category arcs around the circle (re-confirmed by tracing the actual category of each printed axis label in order, accounting for the circle's wraparound rather than assuming a flat left-to-right reading).
- What was NOT AI-generated: all three requests - given directly. The choice to reuse step2b's existing OUTCOME_FEATURES split (rather than a new classification) and the Plotly categoryorder bugfix were both made here, not asked for verbatim.
