## Entry 072 — Section E redesigned: dumbbell chart + tiered verdict, replacing the two bar charts + single miscast score (src/step2b_player_diagnostics.py, src/portal.py, DIAGNOSTICS_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: full spec - "presentation, naming, and auto-text only; the two partial projections and the JS score computation stay exactly as they are." (1) replace the dense caption with one plain-language line, move the feature-split lists into a collapsed "How this works" expander (text unchanged); (2) replace the two side-by-side bar charts with ONE dumbbell chart - all 8 archetypes, no cutoff, sorted by |productive-used| descending, two dots per row connected by a line, hover with both values + signed gap (Python-rounded); (3) a tiered, all-computed verdict - (a) an overall tercile verdict off the league percentile as the headline, (b) a top-role-flip line only when the used/productive top archetypes actually differ, (c) a largest-gap line explicitly subordinated to (a), grounded with one real outcome-feature number and one real opportunity-feature number; (4) rename "Miscast score" -> "Usage-production alignment", lead with the percentile, demote the raw JS value; (5) keep the causal caveat visible, moved under the verdict block; wire the Scouting Summary's miscast clause to the same tiered verdict.
- What was used: added ONE new function to step2b_player_diagnostics.py - `miscasting_feature_grounding()` - which finds the single outcome feature whose z-score most strongly aligns with the underused archetype's own archetypoid z-profile (`z[f] * fit["basis"][archetype, f]`, largest positive product - the same archetypoid-profile reference point `drift_attribution` already uses, extended from a sign-agreement check to a magnitude-ranked product since this needs a single BEST feature, not a qualifying set) and the opportunity feature with the largest deficit against that same profile (`basis[archetype, f] - z[f]`, largest positive value) - `miscasting_score()`/`_partial_projection()` themselves are completely untouched, exactly as the spec required. In portal.py: a new `dumbbell_chart()` helper (raw go.Figure, not `archetype_column_chart`, since that function's 2%-display-floor would hide exactly the near-zero rows a used-vs-productive comparison most needs to show) - one grey connecting-line trace per archetype plus two shared marker traces (clean 2-entry legend), using the dataviz skill's own categorical slots 1/2 (blue/orange, already validated against this app's surface and already in use for Section C1's chart). Found and fixed a real y-axis-ordering gotcha before it shipped: a plain cartesian y-axis's "array" categoryorder places the array's FIRST entry at the BOTTOM (confirmed empirically against this app's own C2 chart, whose ascending-sorted dataframe rendered its largest value at the TOP), so `categoryarray` is built as the REVERSE of the sorted-by-|gap| order, otherwise the single most important row (biggest gap) would render at the bottom instead of the top. The tiered verdict reuses `_stat_gauge_card` verbatim for tier (a) (value_display = the tercile word, the "headline" the spec asks for; its small muted-caption slot carries the demoted raw JS value, read as a faithful interpretation of "demote to hover/expander" since a live per-player number doesn't belong in a static generic expander), card color keyed to the tercile word via this file's existing green/gold/coral hexes (matching every other tercile verdict in the file). Tiers (b)/(c) render as plain markdown/caption lines below the card - visually smaller, matching "explicitly subordinated."
- Verified: killed and restarted the Streamlit dev server, drove it with Playwright against Julius Randle (3-season vet) and Danny Wolf (rookie/1-season) - both render with no exception. Confirmed the dumbbell's sort order visually from the actual screenshot (not inferred from DOM text order): "Inside Scoring Big" (the largest-gap row) renders at the top, "Mobile Big" (near-zero gap) at the bottom, matching the intended sort direction. Confirmed the verdict card, top-role-flip line, and grounded largest-gap line all show real, distinct computed values ("Well aligned", 10%/2% percentiles, "Rim Protector / Roll Man (+11.2pp)" backed by real Free-throw-rate/Putbacks numbers for Randle). Confirmed the "How this works" expander opens and shows the original feature-split text unchanged.
- A real bug found and fixed during verification (not asked for): the top-role-flip sentence read "Deployed primarily as a Inside Scoring Big" - wrong article before a vowel sound. Fixed with a small `article()` lambda checking the archetype name's first letter against AEIOU (safe for this project's fixed set of exactly 8 archetype names, two of which - "Inside Scoring Big" and "Offensive Engine" - actually need "an").
- What was NOT AI-generated: every wording template, the tercile labels, the grounding formula, the rename, and "the two partial projections stay exactly as they are" constraint - all specified directly in the task spec. Catching and fixing the article-grammar bug was done here, from reading the actual rendered sentence, not flagged in the spec.

## Entry 073 — D3 upgraded from a single-teammate split to an 8-archetype role sensitivity profile (src/step2b_player_diagnostics.py, src/portal.py, DIAGNOSTICS_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: full spec - "UPGRADE to the D3 primary view - sensitivity PROFILE instead of a single context: one diverging bar per context archetype (usage-proxy delta, HIGH-minus-LOW teammate exposure to that archetype), grey/hatched under a possession floor; a role-aware auto-verdict that frames 'no significant deltas' as a POSITIVE plug-and-play finding rather than an empty state; demote the old single-teammate context view to a drill-down-only detail; GATE an optional rim-vs-3PT shot-mix elasticity (and assist-rate) on whether shot-distance data exists in cached PBP - if not, state the limitation; run a sanity check against a known ball-dominant guard vs. a known 3&D wing."
- What was used: investigated the actual data before writing any UI - the stints parquet (`off_p1..p5`, `off_fga/fgm/fg3a/fg3m/tov/points`, no assist or shot-location columns) and the cached raw play-by-play (`data/stints/raw_cache/*_pbp.parquet`, NBA's PlayByPlayV3 schema) directly, rather than assuming the "assist-rate" and "rim-vs-3PT" pieces were buildable. Found: shot distance IS cached per shot event, and assist credit exists only as free text inside the play description (e.g. "(Bridges 3 AST)"), never a structured column - but critically, NEITHER is joinable to a specific stint, because stints_*.parquet (the table every D-section split already reads) stores only pre-aggregated per-stint counts with no pointer back to individual play-by-play rows. That join would mean re-deriving a chunk of build_stints.py's own substitution-boundary walk (extensively fixed for edge cases already, per that file's own history) against ~3700 cached games - the identical scope tradeoff role_elasticity's own docstring already declined for individual usage attribution. Built `shot_mix_gate_status()` to check this directly and report the real reason, rather than skip it silently or force a fragile workaround. Added `role_sensitivity_profile()` (step2b): for each of 8 archetypes, computes a per-stint "exposure" score (sum of the 4 on-court teammates' own archetype-c share, from the same recipes.csv every other section reads), splits the player's own on-floor stints by the REAL MEDIAN of that score (not a fixed cutoff), and reuses a newly-factored-out `_summarize_stint_block()` (the exact same 6 metrics role_elasticity already validated, pulled out of that function so both share one implementation) on each half - gated independently per archetype at the same 500-possession floor. In portal.py: a new `sensitivity_profile_chart()` (thin bars render grey + a diagonal pattern fill - color AND texture, not color alone - rather than reusing `diverging_bar`, which has no notion of a per-bar confidence flag and is shared by 2 other call sites) and `_role_sensitivity_verdict()`, with a disclosed fixed 3.0-points/100 "meaningful shift" threshold (not derived from a league distribution, which would need this same per-player stint computation run for ~450 players - a much heavier cost than C1/C2's distributions). The OLD single-teammate view's rendering was fully replaced; `role_elasticity()`/`load_role_elasticity_cached()`/`highest_shared_minutes_teammate()` themselves were left completely untouched, since the paused Report/PDF export feature still calls `load_role_elasticity_cached` directly (same discipline as Entry 071's C1/C2 work).
- Sanity check (run directly against step2b before touching any UI, per the spec's own instruction): Luka Dončić (91% Offensive Engine/Combo Guard, the ball-dominant-guard case) vs. Luguentz Dort (62% 3&D Wing, 14.1 USG% - picked from real 2025-26 data by querying for lowest usage + highest 3PT-share + highest spot-up-play-type share, not assumed). Both produced non-degenerate, fully-available 8-archetype profiles (1407 and 1088 on-floor stints respectively, every archetype split clearing the 500-possession floor on both sides). The result did NOT confirm my own naive prior (that a ball-dominant star would show flatter deltas than a role player) - Dort's single biggest swing (+10.4 pts/100, Offensive-Engine exposure) was actually LARGER than Luka's (+7.8, Shooting-Specialist exposure). Reported this honestly rather than forcing the expected story: what the numbers DO show is a basketball-sensible complementary-fit pattern for both - Luka's offense is helped most by shooting-specialist teammates (floor spacing for a downhill creator) and hurt most by non-shooting bigs (clogged driving lanes); Dort's is helped most by an offensive-engine teammate (a creator generating clean catch-and-shoot looks for a low-usage wing) - a real, checkable, mechanically-sound result, which is what this sanity check was actually verifying (the computation isn't degenerate or random), not a specific magnitude ranking.
- Verified further: launched a fresh Streamlit instance, drove it with Playwright. E.J. Liddell (349 min, 2025-26) correctly renders all 8 archetypes as grey/hatched and the verdict correctly degrades to "every context split is too thin... too thin to say how his environment shapes his production" rather than picking a winner from noise. The drill-down expander opens, defaults to the single biggest-mover archetype, and its 6-metric detail view (reusing the OLD rendering code verbatim, fed the NEW high/low split) matches the possession counts and thin-data warning correctly. Julius Randle (a real Nets player) also renders with no exception.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the profile/verdict/gate/drill-down structure, the median-split mechanic, and "frame near-zero as a positive plug-and-play finding" - all specified directly in the task spec. Investigating whether the assist-rate/shot-mix pieces were ACTUALLY buildable from the real data (rather than assuming the spec's gate would simply pass), and reporting the sanity check's real (not hypothesized) finding, were both done here.

## Entry 074 — D3 rebuild Phase 1: event-to-stint link table (src/build_stints.py)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "Fix D3 properly by closing the data gap it downgraded around... PHASE 1 - EVENT-TO-STINT LINK (extend build_stints.py, additive only - do NOT change the existing stint table or its schema): export an additional per-game table (data/stints/events/{game_id}.parquet): one row per attributed PBP event with game_id, stint_id, actionType, subType, personId, teamId, shotDistance if present, and the on-court flag linking it to the stint. Validate: per stint, the sum of attributed scoring events must reproduce the stint's off_points (print mismatches). Resumable/cached like everything else; rebuild only from the existing raw_cache (no new network pulls)."
- What was used: `build_game_stints()`'s existing per-event walk already resolves, at the exact point it increments fga_count/fgm_count/tov_count for the stint-level table, everything an event row needs (etid, personId, made/missed, shotValue) - the event emission is added in that SAME branch rather than a second pass, tagged `stint_id = stint_num + 1` (stint_num only increments inside `_flush()`, so every event since the last flush belongs to whichever stint is about to be flushed next - identical reasoning the existing counters already rely on). Assist rate needed a new capability: PlayByPlayV3 has no structured assist column (checked live against cached PBP) - only free text at the end of a made shot's `description` (e.g. "(Bridges 3 AST)"). Added `_ASSIST_RE` + `_parse_assist_person_id()`, reusing the EXISTING `_resolve_player_by_name()` the substitution parser already validates (diacritics, "F. Wagner"-style prefix disambiguation, family-name-collision guard) rather than writing a second name-matcher. `process_game()`'s resumability was restructured to check the games file and the NEW events file independently (the whole season was already fully processed before this table existed, so the old "skip if games file exists" gate would have skipped every game before a single events file could be written); `run()`'s manifest fast-path was updated to match. Added `validate_events()` (combines the per-game event files, filtered to the season's own game_ids using the same cross-season-bleed-avoidance pattern `combine_and_validate` already established, then sums made-shot + made-free-throw points per (game_id, stint_id, teamId) and diffs against the existing stints table's off_points).
- A real bug found and fixed during validation (not anticipated up front): `shotResult` is BLANK for every Free Throw row regardless of outcome (confirmed live - `shotValue` is also always 0 on the raw FT row, never 1), so the first version of `made = event["shotResult"] == "Made"` marked every free throw as missed, undercounting points on any stint containing one. Fixed by reading the "MISS " prefix in the FT event's own `description` instead (the same convention already relied on for Made/Missed Shot text elsewhere in this file) - this dropped the mismatch rate on a 5-game test sample from 197/525 rows (37.5%) to 4/274 (1.5%).
- A second finding, investigated but NOT fixed (out of this Phase's scope): running `validate_events()` on the full 2025-26 season (310,863 events across 1230 games, all from cache, ~1m45s, zero network calls) found 662/75,587 directed rows (0.876%) still mismatch, some severely (off_points as extreme as -124 on a 1-possession row). Traced this to a real, PRE-EXISTING characteristic of the already-shipped stint table, not anything this Phase touches: every one of the 198 negative-off_points mismatches co-occurs with `_flush()`'s own already-documented "floored to 1 possession" fallback (a free-throw trip whose points land in one stint while its ending event fires in an adjacent one, from a legal mid-trip substitution) - misattributed points that net to zero over a full game (score reconciliation is still 100.0% exact for all 1230 games, unchanged) but produce a nonsense single-stint value at that exact boundary. Did not attempt to repair `_flush()`'s scoring logic: the task's own "do NOT change the existing stint table" instruction rules it out, and a ~0.9% rate concentrated at one already-documented edge case doesn't undermine the event table itself (the events are correctly personId-attributed; only the coarser stint-level score aggregate they're being cross-checked against has this quirk). Reported plainly via `validate_events()`'s own printed output rather than silently absorbed.
- Verified: ran the full season backfill (`--sleep 0`, justified since every game's box/PBP was already cached from the original run - confirmed zero network calls by timing it: ~1m45s total for 1230 games) and the validation function; spot-checked a real event file's `on_court` flag (234 True / 11 False for one sample game - the small False rate matches the kind of residual unlogged-lineup-change edge case `_period_opening_five`'s own docstring already discloses, confirming the flag is doing real validation work, not just always-True) and its `assistPersonId` column (51 of 78 made shots correctly got a resolved assister; the other 27 unassisted makes correctly show null, not a parse failure).
- What was NOT AI-generated: the schema, the additive-only constraint, the resumability requirement, and the validation requirement - all specified directly in the task spec. The free-throw shotResult bug and the pre-existing stint-anomaly root-cause were both found and diagnosed here, not flagged in the spec.

## Entry 075 — D3 rebuild Phase 2: individual elasticity from real events, replacing the team-ORtg proxy (src/step2b_player_diagnostics.py, src/portal.py, DIAGNOSTICS_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "PHASE 2 - REBUILD D3 AS DESIGNED (individual elasticity, not a team proxy): For each of the 8 context archetypes: split the subject's on-floor POSSESSIONS (not stints) by his 4 teammates' combined exposure to that archetype (median split as now). From the new event table compute HIS OWN behavior per side: usage proxy = his (FGA + TOV + 0.44*FTA) share of his team's events while on floor; assist rate. Report the HIGH-minus-LOW delta per archetype - this replaces the team-ORtg delta entirely. If shotDistance survived into the event table, also report his rim-vs-3PT share delta per context; otherwise state it's unavailable. Retitle back to 'Does his game change with who's on the floor?'... Keep: hatching below the possession floor (but verify the floor against POSSESSION counts, not stint counts...), the role-aware verdict..., the D1-credibility connective line, and the drill-down expander. Sanity checks to print: (a) a high-minute ball-dominant guard must show a negative usage delta on the on-ball-creator context with BOTH sides above the floor...; (b) the removed team-ORtg proxy version: delete its UI; keep the function as superseded-but-visible per project convention, noting it duplicated D2's question."
- What was used: `individual_role_sensitivity_profile()` (step2b) reuses the EXACT SAME median-exposure-split mechanic as the superseded `role_sensitivity_profile()` (kept self-contained rather than factored into a shared helper, so the superseded function stays a complete, independently-readable "kept but not wired up" artifact, not entangled with the new one) - "possessions not stints" is honored by weighting every aggregate by real `off_possessions`/event counts, not stint COUNT (a stint-and-sum-possessions split already IS possession-weighted, since teammate exposure is constant for a whole lineup-window - literally enumerating individual possessions would produce an identical result). Filters the ~311k-row season event table down ONCE to this player's own team's events during his on-floor stints (a small subset) before running the 8-way archetype split, rather than re-scanning the full table 16 times. `_individual_event_summary()` computes usage proxy exactly as specified, assist rate as his-assists-on-any-teammate's-make ÷ his team's total makes (a simple, disclosed formula choice - the spec named the usage-proxy formula exactly but left assist rate's own denominator unstated), and rim/3PT share using this project's own existing "0-3 ft" bucket boundary (RIM_DISTANCE_FT=3) so the new metric reads on the same scale as the archetype model's own shot-profile features. In portal.py: NEW `individual_sensitivity_chart()`/`_individual_sensitivity_verdict()`/`SENSITIVITY_SIGNIFICANT_USAGE_DELTA_PP` (3.0pp, a disclosed fixed threshold analogous to the old 3.0-points/100 one but rescaled for a 0-1 share metric) - the OLD `sensitivity_profile_chart()`/`_role_sensitivity_verdict()`/`SENSITIVITY_SIGNIFICANT_DELTA` are untouched, just no longer called, per (b)'s explicit instruction. The D1-credibility line reads `d1_diff` (threaded as a new parameter into `_render_role_elasticity`, computed once already in `render_section_c` for D1's own chart) and compares D3's single biggest-|usage-delta| archetype against D1's `argmax`, stating alignment or divergence plainly rather than forcing a confirming story. `shot_mix_gate_status()` was updated to check for the Phase 1 events file directly (now resolves `available=True`) rather than re-deriving its own outdated per-column check - the rim/3PT metrics it used to gate are simply always computed now, no separate gate needed in the live UI.
- Sanity check (a), run directly against the computation with formal assertions before touching any UI: Luka Dončić (2025-26, 1407 on-floor stints) shows usage_delta=-8.41pp (Combo Guard context) and -8.37pp (Offensive Engine context) - both negative as required, both sides of both splits comfortably above the 500-possession floor (1481-2954 possessions), neither flagged thin. A low-minute player (E.J. Liddell, 349 min) correctly shows every one of the 8 archetypes thin=True - confirming the floor logic doesn't produce false-confident readings for a genuinely small sample, and doesn't over-flag a real high-minute player either.
- Verified further: launched a fresh Streamlit instance, drove it with Playwright. Luka's live page matches the direct-Python sanity check exactly (same -8.4pp/-8.4pp values, same sorted bar order). The drill-down expander opens, defaults to the single biggest-mover archetype, and shows real rim/3PT/assist percentages with a caption printing BOTH possession and stint counts on each side (e.g. "2954 high-exposure possessions (848 stints), 1481 low-exposure (559 stints)"). Julius Randle (real Nets player) renders with no exception. Confirmed via grep that `sensitivity_profile_chart`/`_role_sensitivity_verdict`/`load_role_sensitivity_cached` (the old team-proxy UI) have zero remaining call sites anywhere in the live render path.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the usage-proxy formula, the possession-not-stint framing, the retitle, the D1-credibility requirement, and the two specified sanity checks - all given directly in the task spec. The assist-rate denominator choice and the rim-distance bucket boundary were judgment calls made here, disclosed as such in DIAGNOSTICS_README.md rather than presented as the only possible definition.

- What was used: `_simple_stat_card()` is a new, smaller sibling of `_stat_gauge_card` (no percentile bar - these 3 tiles are a name+share pair, not a percentile position) built from the same label/value/muted-sub visual language for house-style consistency, reused verbatim across all 3 (or 2, merged) cards. `_select_gap_chart_indices()` is a pure function operating on the `gaps_pp` array alone - verified this never touches `used_top_idx`/`productive_top_idx` (the amendment's own explicit warning) by writing a dedicated synthetic test case: an archetype with near-zero usage but a large production share (a classic "untapped direction," not anyone's top archetype in either projection) that must survive the filter regardless. `usage_production_gap_chart()` reuses `diverging_bar()` itself (not a lookalike) for "deliberately consistent," post-processing the returned figure's own hover/annotations only (the same established pattern C2's drift-attribution chart already uses, so D1/D2/D3's other `diverging_bar` call sites are untouched) - hover customdata is built by replicating `diverging_bar`'s own internal ascending sort on the exact same values, the same discipline applied after the chart/text-mismatch bug found once already this session (Section B's radar chart), so a hover bubble can never label the wrong bar. `dumbbell_chart()` itself is completely unchanged, just moved inside `st.expander("See both full portraits")`. For the grounding-clause fix: added `INTUITIVE_EVIDENCE_FEATURES` (the exact whitelist given, mapped onto this project's real feature names) and `GROUNDING_CONTRIBUTION_THRESHOLD` (0.3 SD, reusing `drift_attribution()`'s own existing cutoff rather than inventing a second number) to step2b; `miscasting_feature_grounding()`'s outcome AND opportunity searches are now BOTH restricted to this same whitelist (not just the outcome side, even though `OPPORTUNITY_FEATURES` itself has no defensive stats - the whitelist also excludes offense-side features judged less "intuitive" as evidence, e.g. isolation/post-up shares, not just anything non-offensive) and each independently returns `None` if nothing whitelisted clears the threshold, letting the caller build a two-sided, one-sided, or fully-omitted sentence.

Danny Wolf (used==produces, merged card) shows "Used and produces as: Combo Guard (23% used · 37% produces)" spanning one card, a separate "Biggest untapped: Combo Guard (+14.5pp)" card (a real, self-consistent coincidence - Combo Guard's OWN gap happens to be the single largest across all 8, not a bug), and a two-sided grounding clause (both TS% and USG% cleared the bar). Hovering the top bar on Randle's chart showed "Rim Protector / Roll Man / used 2% → produces 13% / Gap: 11.24pp" - matching the sentence below exactly, confirming no chart/text mismatch.

## Entry 079 — Report section: per-player PDF scouting report generator (new src/player_report.py; src/portal.py; requirements.txt; DIAGNOSTICS_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "Add a 'Report' section at the very end of the Diagnostic Analysis page: a per-player PDF scouting report generator with two buttons - 'Generate preview'... and 'Download PDF'... New module src/player_report.py for the PDF building; portal.py only wires the UI. CORE RULE: The PDF is assembled ONLY from values, verdict texts, and figures the page has ALREADY computed for the selected player... Never recompute analytics inside the report builder, never hardcode any player-specific text, never fabricate a number that isn't on the page." Full spec covered: header (circular headshot via `hull_callout_chart.get_headshot_data_uri`, bio line, footer with page numbers), a Scouting Summary paragraph, one block per Section A-E (verdict text + one representative chart each, two for D), kaleido export at 2x with a gate-check before wiring, `st.pdf`/iframe preview fallback, session-state caching + Regenerate, and an explicit two-player validation requirement (full-data veteran + thin-data player, confirm graceful degradation not blanks/crashes).
- What was used: One-directional dependency - `portal.py` imports `player_report.py`; `player_report.py` imports neither `portal.py` nor Streamlit, only `hull_callout_chart.get_headshot_data_uri` (already Streamlit-free) - chosen so `portal.py`'s top-level `st.set_page_config`/sidebar code never has to execute as a side effect of importing the PDF module, and so `player_report.py` stays a pure, independently-testable function of a plain dict. `collect_report_data()` (portal.py) builds that dict by calling the exact same cache wrappers and chart functions every live section already uses (`load_mismatch`, `load_teammate_lift`, `load_individual_role_sensitivity_cached`, `load_miscasting_cached`, `archetype_column_chart`, `signature_radar_chart`, `usage_production_gap_chart`, the drift line chart, etc.) - confirmed by construction, not just claimed, since it is the same function objects, not reimplementations. `player_report.build_pdf()` is a pure function (reportlab `SimpleDocTemplate`/`Table`/`Paragraph`, kaleido `fig.to_image()`, PIL circular headshot crop via an alpha mask, reportlab-native `Circle`+`String` vector fallback for the no-photo SVG case rather than adding an SVG-rasterization dependency). `_md_bold_to_reportlab()` escapes XML-unsafe characters (`xml.sax.saxutils.escape`) BEFORE inserting `<b>` tags, not after - fixed a real bug the OLD (pre-this-session) PDF code had, where a bare "&" in a string like "3&D Wing" was misparsed as the start of an XML entity reference. Two genuine spec conflicts were resolved by omission, not fabrication, per the CORE RULE: (1) no Scouting Summary page exists in the PDF, because `render_scouting_summary` was deleted from the live page earlier this same session (Entry ~071) and the CORE RULE forbids fabricating a paragraph that isn't on the page anymore; (2) each section's PDF caveat reuses that section's own existing on-page caveat sentence verbatim, since the old global "Limitations" block that used to house several of these was also removed this session - both are documented in `player_report.py`'s own module docstring, `collect_report_data()`'s comments, and DIAGNOSTICS_README.md's new "Report (PDF export)" section. Deleted (not superseded-and-hidden) the old, already-defunct pre-session PDF export code (`build_player_report_data`, `render_player_report_preview`, `_fig_png`, `build_player_report_pdf`, `render_player_report_export`, ~315 lines) - it referenced field names from before this session's Section E/D3 rebuilds and was fully disconnected from current reality, and the new spec's own "portal.py only wires the UI" architecture is a genuine replacement, not a second implementation to maintain in parallel.
- Two real bugs found by checking actual output, not by assumption: (1) `st.pdf` - `hasattr(st, "pdf")` is True in this Streamlit version (1.56.0), but `st.pdf`'s own docstring requires the `streamlit-pdf` extra, confirmed NOT installed (`import streamlit_pdf` → `ModuleNotFoundError`); an initial `except AttributeError` wouldn't have caught the actual failure (a different exception from inside Streamlit's delta_generator), so it was broadened to `except Exception` guarded by an explicit `hasattr` check first - `streamlit-pdf` was deliberately not installed, since the spec's own fallback chain anticipates exactly this. The fallback itself was then simplified from a manual `<iframe>`-in-`components.html` to `st.iframe(data_uri, height=920)` after noticing Streamlit's own deprecation warning for `components.html` in this exact use ("replace `st.components.v1.html` with `st.iframe`") - only at this one call site, since the app's many OTHER `components.html` uses inject arbitrary HTML (tables, charts) that `st.iframe`'s src-only API can't do. (2) D's two-chart layout - first built side by side at half width (430px) to fit both in one row; rendering both to PNG and looking at them (per this project's own "look at the actual chart, don't assume" convention from the dataviz workflow) showed D1's plain centered axis title shrinks cleanly, but D3's two paper-coordinate end-labels ("cedes the ball" / "takes over more of the offense", sized for a much wider canvas) visually collided into overlapping, unreadable text at 430px. Fixed by stacking both charts vertically at full width (900px) instead of guessing at shorter label text that might still collide at some other width - applied as a general fix to `player_report.py`'s multi-figure layout (vertical stack, not a row), not a one-off special case for D.
- Verified: standalone-smoke-tested `player_report.py` alone first, with synthetic data, before building the larger integration (headshot real-photo + SVG-fallback paths, two-chart layout, bold/escaping, table, footer - all confirmed by reading the rendered PDF). Then, since no browser-automation tool was available in this environment (checked - no `chromium-cli`, no MCP browser tool), used Streamlit's own `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` harness to drive the REAL script (not a mock): set the sidebar nav to Diagnostic Analysis, selected a player via `st.session_state["diag_selected_pid"]` (bypassing the quadrant-chart click that normally sets it, which AppTest can't simulate), clicked the actual "Generate preview" button, and confirmed zero exceptions end-to-end for both required cases: Julius Randle (full-data veteran, all 5 sections populated, both D charts, 513KB PDF) and E.J. Liddell (349 min, the thinnest-data roster player with a recipe - 542KB PDF). Read both generated PDFs directly (not just checked byte counts) to confirm every chart image is non-blank and real: Liddell's PDF correctly shows "Rookie season - no drift history available" with no chart for Section C (nothing to plot from one season - an appropriately-omitted chart with a stated reason, not a blank area) and a fully-hatched D3 chart (all 8 archetypes below the 500-possession floor for him) paired with its own "too thin to say how his own game shifts" text - the same hatching-below-the-floor convention the live D3 chart already uses, confirming the PDF didn't invent a special case for thin data. Confirmed `kaleido`/`reportlab` were already in requirements.txt; added `pillow` (used directly via `from PIL import Image, ImageDraw` for headshot cropping, previously relying on it being pulled in transitively by another dependency rather than declared).
- What was NOT AI-generated: the CORE RULE itself, the full page/section structure, the UI behavior spec (buttons, caching, filename convention), and the explicit two-player validation requirement - all given directly. The one-directional module dependency, the SVG-fallback vector redraw, the decision to omit rather than fabricate the Scouting Summary and per-section caveats, the `AppTest`-based validation approach (substituting for the unavailable browser tool), and the vertical-stack fix for the annotation-collision bug were judgment calls made here to implement the spec safely and verify it honestly.

## Entry 087 — Fixed: PDF preview blocked by Chrome (src/portal.py)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "PDF无法在页面preview This page has been blocked by Chrome, this is information I got from page" - a real bug reported directly from the user's own browser session on the new Player Report page (not something visible from this environment's own tools, which have no browser access - see Entry 079's note on that limitation).
- What was used: root cause is a known Chrome restriction, not a bug specific to this app - Chrome blocks `data:` URIs used directly as an iframe's `src` for PDF content outright, which is exactly what the previous fallback (`st.iframe(f"data:application/pdf;base64,{pdf_b64}")`, Entry 082/084's simplification away from `components.html`) did. Fixed by reverting THIS ONE call site back to `components.html` with a small inline script: the base64 PDF bytes are decoded into a `Uint8Array` and wrapped in a `Blob` client-side, inside the sandboxed iframe `components.html` already renders into, then a nested `<iframe>` is pointed at the resulting `blob:` URL (created via `URL.createObjectURL`) instead of the `data:` URI directly - `blob:` URLs aren't subject to the same Chrome restriction, since they're scoped to the document that created them rather than being the `data:` scheme itself. `st.iframe` wasn't the wrong API choice in principle (this genuinely is a "load a URL" case, and it's kept for the app's OTHER src-only uses) - the specific content type (a `data:` PDF) was what Chrome objected to, and `st.iframe` has no way to run the client-side conversion this fix needs, only `components.html` does.
- Verified: rendered the exact f-string template standalone in Python and printed the resulting HTML/script - confirmed correct base64/CSS substitution and balanced JS braces (2 open, 2 close) before trusting the embedded script's syntax. Confirmed via `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` that the full Generate-and-preview flow still executes with zero Python-side exceptions. Could NOT verify the fix resolves the actual Chrome-side block myself (no browser tool in this environment) - the Blob-URL technique is the standard, well-established workaround for this exact Chrome behavior, but this should be re-confirmed in the user's own browser rather than taken as fully verified from this side alone.
- What was NOT AI-generated: reporting the exact browser error text and that it happened at all - the user's own find.

## Entry 088 — PDF preview STILL blocked by Chrome after Entry 087's fix - switched to page-by-page PNG images (src/portal.py, requirements.txt, DIAGNOSTICS_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "This page has been blocked by Chrome" (reported again, verbatim, after Entry 087's Blob-URL fix was already deployed) - meaning that fix did not actually resolve the real problem, only a hypothesis about it.
- What was used: recognized that Entry 087's approach (and the one before it) both succeed at the PYTHON level unconditionally - `components.html`/`st.iframe` return normally regardless of what the browser does with the HTML/JS content they're given - so wrapping either one in a `try/except` can never detect a browser-side rendering failure and step to a further fallback; the two "layers" of the original fallback chain were never actually reachable as a chain once past `st.pdf`. Rather than attempt a 3rd guess at Chrome's specific iframe/sandbox/PDF-viewer restrictions with no way to test them in this environment, switched to the ORIGINAL spec's own explicitly-anticipated last-resort fallback: "if neither renders reliably, show page-by-page PNG previews instead." At Generate-time (inside the button handler, not the preview render itself), the built PDF is rasterized via `pdf2image.convert_from_bytes` (a thin wrapper around `poppler`'s `pdftoppm`/`pdftocairo` binaries, confirmed already present on this machine via `which`) at 150 DPI; each page's PNG bytes are cached alongside the PDF bytes in the same session-state dict (`page_pngs`, a new key). The preview code now tries `st.pdf` first (unchanged), then on failure loops `st.image()` over the cached page PNGs - a plain image tag, not a PDF viewer, so no PDF-specific browser restriction is left to trigger. Removed the now-dead Blob-URL/iframe code and the `import base64` it needed (confirmed via grep it had no other use in the file); added `import io` (page-PNG byte buffering) and `import pdf2image`.
- A real environment bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to the Chrome issue itself but blocking verification of it: `pip install pdf2image` (using whatever `pip` was on PATH) silently installed into a miniconda Python 3.11 environment, while the actual running `streamlit` process (and every test script this session has used via a plain `python3` invocation) runs on a DIFFERENT interpreter, `/usr/local/bin/python3` (Python 3.10.9 - confirmed via the `streamlit` executable's own shebang line and by comparing `sys.executable`/version strings directly, not assumed). Fixed by installing explicitly with `/usr/local/bin/python3 -m pip install pdf2image` instead of relying on whichever `pip` happened to resolve first on PATH - a distinction worth remembering for any future dependency install in this project's remaining time, since a normal `pip install` here silently installs into a DIFFERENT Python (3.11 conda) than the one Streamlit actually runs (3.10 framework build), which would look successful and then still fail with a real `ModuleNotFoundError` at runtime.
- Verified: gate-checked `pdf2image` directly before wiring it up (this project's own established convention for any new rendering dependency, same as kaleido's own gate-check in Entry 079) - converted a real generated PDF (Michael Porter Jr., 3 pages) to images, read page 1's PNG directly (confirmed sharp, correctly laid out, matching the PDF's actual content, not blank or corrupted) before trusting it further. Timed the conversion (~0.43s for 3 pages) to confirm caching it at generation time - rather than recomputing on every page rerun - was actually worth doing, not just precautionary. Confirmed via `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` (using the correct `/usr/local/bin/python3` interpreter this time) that the full Generate flow still executes with zero exceptions and `page_pngs` (3 entries) lands correctly in the cached session-state dict. Could not verify `st.image` specifically renders via AppTest's own introspection (no `.image`-named accessor exists in this Streamlit version's testing API, the same category of gap already hit with `.plotly_chart`/`.download_button` in Entry 079) - relying on the zero-exception result plus the directly-viewed page-PNG correctness instead. As with Entries 087 and before it, could NOT verify this actually resolves the browser-side block myself (no browser tool in this environment) - unlike the two previous, REMOVED attempts, this one has no PDF-viewer-specific browser restriction left to trigger by construction (a plain image, not a PDF embed), which is a stronger basis for confidence than the previous fixes had, but should still be confirmed in the user's own browser rather than assumed.
- What was NOT AI-generated: reporting that the previous fix still didn't work, in the user's own exact words - the user's own find.

REPLACE the two per-transition diverging bar panels with ONE feature-trajectory line chart, deliberately mirroring C1's form... Merge the two auto-attribution text blocks into one block... BUGFIX (real, visible in production): the attribution text renders 'Usage rate (2020% -> 3050%)'... Audit the raw-value formatter per feature... Check every feature's stored unit against real values."
- What was used: (1) Chart swap - new `feature_trajectory_chart()` in `portal.py` plus `diag2b.feature_trajectory()` (new), which z-scores a specific feature set against the basis's fixed mu/sd across ALL of a player's seasons (not just one transition's two endpoints, unlike the existing `drift_attribution()` it otherwise mirrors) - so a feature flagged as a top mover in only ONE of two transitions still gets its full season history plotted. `render_section_b`'s C2 block now computes `attrs` (one `drift_attribution` result per transition, computation fully unchanged) and takes the UNION of both transitions' top-5 feature names (deduped, order-preserving), rendering one combined chart plus one merged text block (one sentence per transition + the "one lens, not causation" caveat ONCE, not once per column). (2) The bugfix - added `ALREADY_PERCENT_FEATURES = {"USG%", "AST%", "TOV%", "STL%", "BLK%", "TRB%"}` as a third bucket in `format_raw_feature_value()` (previously only two: plain-number features and "everything else as a 0-1 fraction") - these 6 are already percentage-points, not fractions, so the old code multiplied them by 100 a second time. Classified by pulling every one of the 29 basis features' real 2025-26 population values (min/max/mean/median) directly rather than assuming - confirmed `USG%` mean≈19.1 range 8.5-38.1 (clearly percent-points already), matching this project's own prior classification of the same 6 features in `render_player_stats_tab`'s `adv_defs` (a different table on the page that had already gotten this right) - the C2 formatter just never matched it.
- Two real problems found by rendering the new chart and looking at it, not assumed correct from the code alone: (1) label collision - the union of both transitions' top movers can reach 9 features for a real player (confirmed - Julius Randle and Michael Porter Jr. both hit exactly 9), and placing each direct end-label at its line's exact data position produced multiple overlapping, unreadable labels (e.g. "Isolation"/"Usage rate", "Post-ups"/"Scoring rate" stacked on top of each other). Fixed with a new `_declutter_label_positions()` - a greedy vertical spacer (lowest-to-highest, enforcing a minimum gap, only moving the LABEL position, never the line's own true data point) with `min_gap` set to ~9% of the chart's y-range, tuned by rendering several real players' charts (both 9-feature and 5-feature unions) and checking for remaining overlap or, conversely, excessive unwarranted spacing - not picked once and left unverified. The y-axis range is widened to fit the adjusted label positions so nothing clips. (2) color reuse - 9 features exceeds the 8-color validated categorical palette, silently repeating a hue (confirmed: "Usage rate" and "Isolation" both rendered blue). Per this project's own dataviz convention ("a 9th series is never a generated hue"), the 9th+ feature keeps its color but switches to a dashed line instead of solid, restoring visual distinctness.
- Verified: confirmed the exact reported bug's real values directly - `format_raw_feature_value("USG%", 20.2)` now returns `"20.2%"` (was `"2020%"`) - then confirmed via live `AppTest` that Michael Porter Jr.'s real biggest-mover sentence now reads "**Usage rate** (20.2% → 30.5%, +1.86 SD)", the exact case originally reported, now correct. Verified all other feature types still format correctly post-fix (`TS%` -> fraction, `PLAYTYPE_SPOTUP` -> fraction, `BPM`/`PTS_PER_100` -> plain number). Rendered the actual trajectory chart to PNG for a 9-feature-union case (Randle), a different 9-feature-union case (MPJ), and a 5-feature/2-season case, confirming: labels cleanly separated with no overlap in the crowded case, appropriately light spacing (not over-spread) in the uncrowded case, exactly 2 points per line for the 2-season player as required, and the dash-pattern distinguishing the repeated-hue 9th feature in both 9-feature cases. Ran `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` across 4 real cases (Randle/MPJ - 3 seasons; a 2-season player found by scanning the wider league population; a rookie/insufficient-data player) - zero exceptions in all 4, and confirmed via `at.caption` (not `at.markdown`, which doesn't capture `st.caption` elements) that the closing caveat appears exactly ONCE per player, not once per transition.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the chart-form swap requirement (mirror C1, union of movers, direct labels, no legend), the text-merge requirement, and the bug report itself (exact wrong text quoted, plus the instruction to check real values rather than assume one rule) - all given directly. The declutter algorithm, its min_gap heuristic, and the dashed-line fallback for a 9th+ series were judgment calls made here to make the requested design actually render correctly, found by looking at real output rather than trusting the code path alone.

## Entry 103 — CRITICAL BUGFIX: raw HTML source was rendering as literal visible text on Purity/Entropy/Role Elasticity/Miscast Risk (src/portal.py)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: pasted a screenshot showing literal `<div style="position:absolute; left:50%; top:-17px; transform:translateX(-50%);">Median</div>` text visibly rendering on the page instead of being parsed as HTML - the "Median"/"Player" overlay added in Entry 099 was broken in the real browser, invisible from this environment's own tools.
- Root cause, confirmed rather than guessed: `_stat_gauge_card()`'s `overlay` variable (added in Entry 099) was built as a triple-quoted, INDENTED multi-line f-string, then spliced into the outer template at an already-indented `{overlay}` position. Reproduced this exact failure mode directly using `markdown_it` (the same markdown-parsing package already installed in this Streamlit environment as a dependency, not a random substitute) - confirmed the assembled HTML rendered as an escaped `<pre><code>` block instead of real tags. The mismatched indentation/blank-line seam where the two multi-line strings joined is a known CommonMark trigger ("indented content after a blank line = an indented code block").
- What was used: rebuilt `_stat_gauge_card()`'s ENTIRE return value (not just the one spot that visibly broke) as single-line, concatenated f-strings with zero embedded newlines anywhere - eliminating this whole class of risk rather than patching the one symptom, since the same mismatched-indentation trap could just as easily hide in a future variant of this function. Re-verified the fixed version through the same `markdown_it` reproduction - confirmed it now parses to real `<div>` tags, no escaped HTML.
- A note on the limits of this verification: `markdown_it` (Python) is not a perfect stand-in for Streamlit's actual browser-side renderer - it separately flagged `_simple_stat_card()`'s existing, already-proven-safe-in-production template (unchanged, not part of this bug) as "broken" too, which real screenshots throughout this session already contradict. So a PASS on this test is a meaningful, not absolute, signal, and a FAIL doesn't automatically mean a real bug - `_simple_stat_card`/`_elasticity_chip_html` were checked but deliberately left untouched, since there's no actual reported or observed breakage in either, and changing them on the strength of an imperfect test alone would have been unwarranted churn. Confirmed via `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` that the fix introduces zero exceptions. Could not get a real browser screenshot to visually confirm the fix myself (no browser tool in this environment, same limitation noted repeatedly this session) - please reload and confirm the cards now render as styled cards, not raw code.
- What was NOT AI-generated: catching the bug at all - the user's own screenshot, since this class of failure (valid Python, valid HTML string, but broken once interpreted as Markdown) is invisible from source code alone and was never going to be caught by this environment's own tools (no browser, and `AppTest` only captures the raw string passed to `st.markdown`, not how a browser's markdown renderer would actually interpret it).

## Entry 105 — Rookie Slot Query: "Recommended units around him" (4-man complement scoring + cards), gap chart moved to a collapsed expander (src/portal.py, DIAGNOSTICS_README.md)
- Date: 2026-07-30
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (full, verbatim): "Add an execution-level 'Recommended units around him' block to the Rookie Slot Query, replacing the gap chart as the primary output (move the gap chart into a collapsed expander 'The environment math behind these picks' - unchanged). COMPUTATION (reuse existing pieces; no new data): Target = the typical-environment archetype vector already computed for the current hypothesis (similarity_weighted_benchmark's all_baseline). Enumerate ALL C(16,4)=1820 four-man complements from the recipe-holding Nets roster. For each: combined environment = mean of the 4 players' recipe alphas. Score each unit on three axes: (a) environment match = 1 - JS distance(combined environment, target) [primary sort]; (b) talent = sum of the 4 players' BPM (existing bpm lookup); (c) structural flags (hard checks, thresholds in DIAGNOSTICS_README.md): rim protection (combined Rim Protector/Roll Man + Mobile Big exposure below threshold -> flag 'no rim protection'); spacing (combined Shooting Specialist + 3&D Wing exposure below threshold -> flag 'spacing risk'); ball dominance (combined Offensive Engine + Combo Guard exposure above threshold -> flag 'second on-ball engine - clashes with the hypothesis role'). Rank: flag-free units first by environment match; break near-ties (within a documented epsilon) by talent. UI: Top 3-5 unit CARDS: Brown's headshot + the 4 teammates' headshots (existing helpers), environment-match score, total BPM, and a checklist line (rim protection Y/N, spacing Y/N, lone-engine Y/N with the key name, e.g. 'rim protection: Claxton'). One contrast card: the WORST flag-heavy unit ('what not to run'), flags listed. All cards recompute live with the hypothesis sliders and the sensitivity variants. Honesty line under the cards: 'Ranked by environment fit + talent under structural constraints - descriptive construction, not a lineup outcome prediction (see the Future Work page for why outcome prediction was ruled out).' Cache per hypothesis vector. Log in AI_USAGE.md; document scoring + thresholds in DIAGNOSTICS_README.md."
- What was used: Reused, not reimplemented: `step4_roster_construction.enumerate_roster_combos()` (already builds the exact combo table needed - player_ids, mean arch_0..arch_{k-1} mixture, sum_bpm - for the Lineup Rankings page's combo_size=5 path; called here with combo_size=4, confirmed to produce exactly 1,820 rows) and `load_bpm_lookup()` (already existed at module level for that same page, unchanged). New: `load_recommended_units_cached()` (JS-distance environment match via `scipy.spatial.distance.jensenshannon`, the 3 structural flags, and the chained-epsilon tie-break-by-talent ranking - full formula and every threshold's real-data calibration written up in DIAGNOSTICS_README.md's new "Rookie Slot Query" section, not reproduced here), `_unit_key_players()` (per-check "key name" attribution from each teammate's own recipe row), `_checklist_item_html()`/`_recommended_unit_card_html()` (card HTML, built as flat single-line f-strings throughout - deliberately following Entry 103's fix rather than risking the same CommonMark-escaping bug in new code), and `_last_name()` (see bug below). `render_rookie_slot_query()` restructured: the new cards render first (primary output), the original gap-chart code moved verbatim into `with st.expander("The environment math behind these picks")` with no logic changes, and the pre-existing sensitivity-variants section was left exactly where and as it was.
- Verified: Ran the full scoring pipeline directly (bare script, not just reasoning about the code) against a real hypothesis before writing any threshold into portal.py, reading real percentiles off all 1,820 real combos to pick UNIT_RIM_SHARE_THRESHOLD/UNIT_SPACING_SHARE_THRESHOLD/UNIT_BALL_DOMINANCE_THRESHOLD/UNIT_ENV_MATCH_EPSILON (none guessed - see DIAGNOSTICS_README.md for the actual percentile table and flag rates). Then exercised the live app with `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest`: zero exceptions loading the page and switching between all 3 rookies (Brown, Bilodeau, and Jefferson - the latter two exercise a different selectbox-default code path than Brown); confirmed the top card's environment-match value changes when the "Primary weight" slider moves (92%->91%), proving the block is live off the hypothesis rather than frozen from first render; pulled the actual rendered card HTML out of AppTest's markdown elements and cross-checked the numbers (rosters, env-match %, BPM, which flags fired) against the same bare-script computation - they matched exactly, including the epsilon tie-break correctly promoting a higher-BPM, slightly-lower-fit combo (Sharpe/Randle/Minott/Etienne) above the raw-best-fit combo (Wolf/Clowney/Randle/Johnson). This process caught one real bug pre-ship: teammate/rookie photo captions used `name.split()[-1]` for a last name, which mis-labels every suffixed name as the suffix itself ("Mikel Brown Jr." rendered as "Jr.", and "Michael Porter Jr." would too on any card including him) - visible directly in the pulled HTML, not spotted by reading the code. Fixed with `_last_name()` (strips trailing Jr./Sr./II/III/IV/V before taking the last word) and reran AppTest to confirm real last names now render.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the entire computation spec (target vector, combo enumeration, the exact 3-axis scoring design, the flag-free-first-then-epsilon-tiebreak ranking rule, the "worst unit" contrast card, the card UI spec, the honesty line's exact wording, and "cache per hypothesis vector") - given directly and in full by the user, quoted verbatim above. Judgment calls made here, within that spec: the specific threshold/epsilon values and how each was calibrated (percentile-based against real data, not guessed); defining "near-tie" as a chained epsilon-from-leader grouping rather than a fixed bucket grid; choosing "most simultaneous flags, then worst fit" (not "single worst fit") for the contrast card, since that reads as a more illustrative "what not to run"; the asymmetric key-name display (shown on a pass for rim/spacing, shown on a fail for lone-engine, since that's the one check where a specific player IS the problem); N_RECOMMENDED_UNITS=4 in a 2x2 grid as the concrete pick within the spec's stated "3-5" range.

Phase 5: full `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` regression across every nav page with zero exceptions (no browser-automation tool is available in this environment), including the Diagnostic Analysis page where the report auto-generates; re-measured real timing rather than assuming the new pipeline is faster - confirmed ≈3.4s per newly-viewed player (down from the old reportlab+kaleido pipeline's measured ≈13.2s, since Playwright renders every chart as inline SVG in one browser pass instead of a separate kaleido subprocess call per chart). This same full-page regression pass also surfaced one real, PRE-EXISTING bug entirely unrelated to this PDF work - the "🎯 Building Around Rookie" nav page's player-selector scoping check still referenced its old pre-rename label ("🎯 Rookie Slot Query"), so `selected_player` was never assigned and the page threw a `NameError` on every visit. Fixed as a one-line label correction since it was trivial, unambiguous, and directly in front of me during verification; noted explicitly as out-of-scope-but-fixed rather than silently bundled in.

## Entry 112 — Fixed 38.7s cold-start latency: exposure-cache disk-persist + precompute-to-file (src/portal_shared.py, src/pipeline/precompute_exposure_cache.py)

- Date: 2026-07-31
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "我发现每次初次加载的时候速度都会特别慢 怎么解决这个问题" (every first load is especially slow, how to fix), then a follow-up mid-task, "如果我后续是要部署怎么解决这个问题" (if I'm going to deploy this later, how do I solve this problem).
- What was used: Timed every cold-start loader directly (not assumed) - `import portal_shared` 1.46s, `load_static()` 0.01s, `load_exposure_cache()` **36.82s**, `load_nets_roster()` 0.37s, `load_player_bio()` 0.00s; total 38.66s. One function accounted for 95% of the cost. Two layers, cheapest-first: (1) added `persist="disk"` to `load_exposure_cache`'s existing `@st.cache_data` decorator - `st.cache_data`'s default cache is in-memory-only, so a running dev server was already paying this cost exactly once (not once per page view), but any restart (or code-change auto-reload) wiped it, which is what "every first load" was actually describing; the return value (`pids`, `exposures`) is a plain picklable tuple, safe to persist, and Streamlit's disk cache lives under `~/.streamlit/cache` (outside this repo, confirmed directly - nothing to gitignore). (2) For the deployment follow-up specifically: `persist="disk"` isn't robust to a fresh container/redeploy with no guaranteed-persistent filesystem, so added `src/pipeline/precompute_exposure_cache.py` (same "precompute offline, ship the file" convention already used for `precompute_hull_bases.py` and `data/basis_2025_26/recipes.csv` itself) - runs `compute_all_player_exposures()` once and saves `(pids, exposures, k)` to `data/basis_2025_26/exposure_cache_2025-26.npz`. `load_exposure_cache()` now checks for this file first (and that its saved `k` matches the caller's `k`, falling through to a real computation if not - a stale/mismatched file should never silently return the wrong shape) and returns near-instantly when present, falling back to the original live computation (still `persist="disk"`-cached) when it's missing, so a fresh clone that hasn't run the precompute script isn't broken, just slower once.
- Verified: `python3 -m py_compile` clean on both files. Ran the precompute script for real (433 players, matching the live run's own player count) and confirmed the resulting file is bitwise-identical in content to a fresh live computation (`np.array_equal` on the exposures array, exact pid-order match) - not just fast, still correct. Re-timed cold: `load_exposure_cache()` with the precomputed file present now takes 0.003s (was 36.82s); a full cold `AppTest` load of the whole app (import + all top-level prep) dropped from an untimed-but-clearly-~39s baseline to **2.97s**. `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` across all 3 visible tabs - zero exceptions, unchanged from before this fix.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the diagnosis request and the deployment-specific follow-up question - both given directly. The two-layer fix itself (which lever solves the dev-server case vs. which solves the deployment case, and that both are needed rather than just one) was proposed here based on the measured timing and this project's own existing precompute convention, not assumed from generic Streamlit-performance advice.

## Entry 115 — Deployment: filtered nets_synergy_deploy.db fallback so a git-clone-only deploy (e.g. Streamlit Community Cloud) has a working database (src/step0_data.py, src/pipeline/build_deploy_db.py, .gitignore)

- Date: 2026-07-31

And the portal doesn't need original db file right? I can just push necessay file on github right?" - then, after the size finding below: "Yes please" (to building the filtered db + gitignore exception).
- What was used: Did not assume the answer - traced every real query against `nets_synergy.db`'s `lineups` table across all 5 live-imported files plus the hidden Rookie Slot Query tab (`step4_roster_construction.py`'s `build_lineup_table`, confirmed reachable only from already-dead code per Entry 111's own findings). Every live call site (`load_mismatch`/`load_teammate_lift` in both `step3_player_breakdown.py` and `step4_report.py`, `load_player_pairs`/`macro_archetype_exposure` feeding `load_exposure_cache`) hardcodes or defaults to `SEASON="2025-26"`, `GROUP_QUANTITY=2` - never any of the other 3 group sizes or 8 other seasons pulled for the (offline-only) synergy-matrix/RAPM work. Measured concretely, not estimated: `lineups` alone is ~200MB of the db's 211MB (confirmed via a real drop-table-and-VACUUM test); filtering it to just the live-queried slice (5,473 of 630,423 rows) and re-VACUUMing produced a real 13.0MB file. Built `src/pipeline/build_deploy_db.py` (copies the real db, filters `lineups` to `GROUP_QUANTITY=2 AND SEASON='2025-26'`, VACUUMs, reports real before/after sizes - fails loudly rather than silently re-filtering if the full db isn't present, so it can't accidentally run against its own already-filtered output) to reproduce this as a repeatable build step. Added a fallback in `step0_data.py`'s `DB_PATH`: uses the real `nets_synergy.db` when present (every current dev/self-hosted environment - zero behavior change there), falls back to the filtered `nets_synergy_deploy.db` only when the real file is absent - which is exactly the state a fresh `git clone`-based deploy would be in, since the real 211MB file is (correctly) gitignored and can't be pushed to GitHub without Git LFS regardless (>100MB single-file hard limit) even if it were meant to be tracked. Added one `.gitignore` negation (`!data/nets_synergy_deploy.db`) so this one ~13MB file is trackable despite the existing blanket `data/**/*.db` exclusion that (correctly) still hides the real 211MB working file.
- Verified: `python3 -m py_compile` clean on both changed files. Ran the real build script - printed real row counts (630,423 -> 5,473) and real file sizes (220.8MB -> 13.0MB), matching the earlier manual test. Directly proved the fallback works, not just plausible: moved the real `nets_synergy.db` aside, confirmed `step0_data.DB_PATH` resolved to the deploy file, ran `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` across all 3 live nav tabs - zero exceptions on the 13MB db alone - then restored the real file and re-ran the same AppTest sweep to confirm normal operation is completely unaffected by the fallback existing. `git check-ignore`/`git add -n` dry-run confirmed the gitignore exception behaves as intended (deploy db stages cleanly; real db stays excluded).
- What was NOT AI-generated: the deployment question itself, the choice between the two presented options (self-hosted vs. Community Cloud) deferred back with a request to verify feasibility first, and the final go-ahead to build the filtered-db approach specifically - all the user's own calls. The exact size/query-scope finding (which table, which rows, 211MB->13MB) was determined here through direct measurement, not assumed or estimated from the schema alone.

## Entry 116 — Fixed real deploy crash: missing data/stints/*.parquet, plus a genuine graceful-degradation gap it exposed (.gitignore, src/step2b_player_diagnostics.py)

- Date: 2026-07-31
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: the user's actual deployed app crashed - "FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: '/mount/src/nets-lineup/data/stints/stints_2025_26.parquet'" - then, after the user had already removed the blanket `data/stints/` exclusion from `.gitignore` themselves to work around it: "这个问题应该是data的stint传到github上 重写.gitignore帮我把需要的数据可以重新上传" (this should be fixed by getting data/stints uploaded to GitHub - rewrite .gitignore to let the needed data be re-uploaded).
- What was used: Entry 115's deployment audit had only traced SQL queries against `nets_synergy.db`'s `lineups` table - it missed that `step2b_player_diagnostics.py` (live, imported by `step3_player_breakdown.py` as `diag2b`) reads `data/stints/*.parquet` FILES directly, for Section D3 "Usage Elasticity" (`individual_role_sensitivity_profile`, reachable from both the live Player Breakdown page and the PDF report - confirmed by tracing its real call chain, not assumed). The user's own `.gitignore` edit (deleting `data/stints/` entirely) would have worked but uploads the full ~296MB directory - traced exactly which 2 files this one live function actually reads (`stints_2025_26_v2.parquet` falling back to `stints_2025_26.parquet`, plus `events_2025_26.parquet`) and replaced the blanket removal with `data/stints/*` + 3 explicit `!` exceptions (~4MB total) - keeping `raw_cache/`, `games/`, and the other 2 seasons' stints (never read by anything live) excluded. Used `data/stints/*`, not `data/stints/`, deliberately - git's negation rule can't re-include a file once its PARENT DIRECTORY itself already matched an exclude pattern, only a glob-on-contents form allows the later `!` lines to take effect. Also fixed the actual root cause while in this code: `_load_season_stints_v2_or_v1()` picked between the v1/v2 stint paths but never checked that EITHER file actually exists before an unconditional `pd.read_parquet()` - unlike its own sibling check for the events file two lines below in every caller. Added the missing existence check (returns `(None, path)`) and a matching guard at all 4 call sites (`role_elasticity`, `role_sensitivity_profile`, `individual_role_sensitivity_profile`, `player_game_ids`) so a future missing-file case degrades to the existing `{"available": False, "reason": ...}` pattern instead of an uncaught crash.
- Verified: `python3 -m py_compile` clean. `git check-ignore` + `git add -n` confirmed, file-by-file, that exactly the 3 needed files are trackable and everything else under `data/stints/` (raw_cache, games, events/ dir, other seasons, manifest.json, sample CSVs) stays excluded. Reproduced the actual crash condition directly: moved all 3 stint files aside, ran `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest` on the Player Breakdown page - confirmed the NEW code degrades gracefully (zero exceptions) where the OLD code would have thrown exactly the user's reported error. Restored the files, called `individual_role_sensitivity_profile` directly - `available: True` with real profile data, confirming the feature actually works, not just fails safely. Re-ran the full 3-tab `AppTest` regression with everything in its normal state - zero exceptions.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the crash report and the initial fix attempt (removing `data/stints/` from `.gitignore` entirely) - both the user's own. The decision to scope it down to the 3 specific files, and the discovery of the missing-existence-check bug underneath it, were determined here by tracing the actual code path the traceback pointed at, not assumed from the error message alone.

## Entry 117 — Player Breakdown gets a sidebar player-picker dropdown, alongside (not replacing) the existing chart-click/table-click selection (src/portal.py, src/step3_player_breakdown.py)

- Date: 2026-07-31
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt (verbatim): "最后一个修改的地方 我想到 在Player Breakdown 添加一个list可以选择球员 就像 Report Tab那样" (one last change - add a list to select the player on Player Breakdown, like the Report tab has).
- What was used: Diagnostic Analysis already had TWO click-driven selection inputs writing into `st.session_state["diag_selected_pid"]` (the quadrant chart's `on_select`, the screening table's photo-click via a one-shot `st.query_params["diag_pid"]` - see that function's own earlier notes). Adding a plain `st.selectbox` as a third input risked either fighting those two (a stale dropdown value clobbering a fresh chart/table click) or lagging a rerun behind them (a fresh chart/table click not visually reaching the dropdown until the next interaction). Root cause: a keyed Streamlit widget's displayed value is fixed at the exact point in the script where it's instantiated, and portal.py's sidebar renders BEFORE `render_diagnostic_analysis` is ever called - so any "sync the dropdown to match a fresh click" logic placed inside that function would always be one rerun late. Resolved by placing a read-only replica of the SAME chart/table-click detection (same two state reads, same table-wins-over-chart priority) in portal.py's sidebar block, pre-syncing the dropdown's `st.session_state` value to match a fresh click BEFORE the widget renders - so whichever of the three inputs actually changed on a given rerun, the other two are never stale. The resolved pid (`selected_diag_pid`) is passed into `render_diagnostic_analysis` as a new `sidebar_pid` parameter, applied last/authoritatively in its existing resolution chain. Followed the Report tab's own sidebar-selectbox precedent exactly (same `roster` population, same `DEFAULT_DIAG_PLAYER_ID` default, same "Player" label) per the user's explicit "like the Report tab" reference.
- Verified: `python3 -m py_compile` clean on both files. `streamlit.testing.v1.AppTest`: (1) default load - sidebar selectbox present, defaulted correctly to "Michael Porter Jr."; (2) switched the dropdown to a different real player (Ben Saraf) - zero exceptions, his name confirmed present on the rendered page; (3) the actual bidirectional-sync case that motivated the whole design - simulated a table-photo click via `at.query_params["diag_pid"]` (Julius Randle's real PLAYER_ID), re-ran, and confirmed the sidebar dropdown updated to "Julius Randle" with zero lag and zero exceptions, proving the pre-sync logic actually works, not just compiles. Full 3-tab regression after - zero exceptions, confirming the other two tabs (untouched by this change) are unaffected.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the request itself and the explicit "like the Report tab" placement/style reference - given directly. The pre-sync-in-the-sidebar design (and the specific insight that the alternative - syncing inside render_diagnostic_analysis - would lag a rerun behind, given Streamlit's widget-instantiation-order behavior) was worked out here by tracing exactly when each of the three inputs' state becomes available relative to when each piece of code runs, not assumed to "just work" from a naive first attempt.

## Entry 118 — Fixed a real deploy failure: `chromium` in requirements.txt isn't a real PyPI package; added a system-Chromium fallback for the PDF export (requirements.txt, packages.txt, src/player_report.py)

- Verified: `python3 -m py_compile` clean. Ran the real PDF pipeline end-to-end locally (Playwright's own managed Chromium, already installed here) - unchanged, still produces a real PDF, confirming the fallback addition didn't disturb the existing working path. Control row (Michael Porter Jr.) confirmed unchanged: solid bars, no pill, 67/18/13 as before. Full `tests/` suite 113/113 passing after the change.

## Entry 141 — Draft Class 2026 landing page (src/step6_draft_class.py, portal.py nav wiring, src/step5_rookie_projections.py axis-label fix, tests/test_draft_class_page.py)

I think it looks too complicated right now, I think we can show three rookie project receipt at the top. But in our model, i think it would be good if we also see their how rookie transfer? ... give me a plan how you think we can work on it" — followed by an explicit scope decision from the owner (via a presented set of options): a NEW landing page with every existing page kept exactly as-is, and the per-player college→NBA translation as the "how it transfers" story (the naive-baseline, holdout-accuracy, and model-limitations stories were offered and declined for this page).
- What was used: a new `src/step6_draft_class.py` (~270 lines) wired into `portal.py` as the FIRST nav entry, so it becomes the default landing page. Three sections: (1) "What we drafted" — three cards, each ending in one bold line (`55% Combo Guard`) so a coach can read only that row and have the answer; (2) "How each rookie translates" — per player, the measured college recipe and the projected NBA recipe side by side with a plain-language confidence verdict; (3) "How to read this" — style-not-talent, confidence varies, the two charts are in different spaces, and what the model cannot see. The page composes step5's already-validated `load_labels()` and `archetype_bar_chart()` rather than reimplementing them (deliberate: `load_nba_labels()` returns a dict-of-dicts, and a reimplementation that forgets to flatten it renders a literal `{'label': ...}` — a bug this project already hit once). Cards are ordered by projection confidence, not file order.
- Design decision worth recording: the page does NOT auto-classify a rookie's move as "role survives" vs "role changes". Doing so would require asserting a semantic equivalence between two archetype spaces that were fit separately on different feature sets — which is exactly the naive-relabeling move this project's own T4 baseline tested and disproved. Instead the confidence read (`Concentrated` ≥45% / `Split` ≥30% / `Diffuse` <30%) is derived purely from the top projected weight, a property of the output itself, and both recipes are shown so the reader draws the comparison.
- Bug found and fixed while verifying: the shared `archetype_bar_chart()` hardcoded its x-axis title to "predicted weight". On this page's COLLEGE chart that number is measured, not predicted — mislabelling the one distinction the page exists to draw. Added an `xaxis_title` parameter defaulting to the old value (so every existing step5 call site is byte-identical in behaviour) and passed "measured weight" for the college side.
- Verified: rendered the real `render_draft_class_page()` offline with captured Streamlit calls — all 3 cards and 6 charts present, headline projections matching the frozen CSV exactly (Brown 55% Combo Guard / Bilodeau 35% Shooting Specialist / Jefferson 24% 3&D Wing), confidence verdicts Concentrated/Split/Diffuse as designed. Confirmed both axis titles at the figure-object level after the fix. 13 new tests in `tests/test_draft_class_page.py` (AppTest confirming Draft Class is the DEFAULT selected page, a guard that every pre-existing nav page still exists, frozen-file agreement, simplex validity of both recipes, confidence-threshold boundaries, graceful degradation on a missing artifact, and a no-write-path grep guard); full suite 126/126 passing. Live-browser screenshot confirmed the landing page and the side-by-side translation section render as intended.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the observation that the portal reads as too complicated for a front-office reader, the decision to lead with the three rookie projections, the request to also show how the rookie "transfers", and the scope choice (new page, keep everything else) — all the owner's own calls. The three-section structure and the refusal to auto-assert a college↔NBA role equivalence were this session's engineering judgment.
