## Entry 143 — Apply the rookie-archetype-cards design handoff to NCAA Bridge section 1 (src/step5_rookie_projections.py, tests/)

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: a design handoff bundle given directly (`design_handoff_rookie_archetype_cards/`: `README.md`, `Rookie Archetype Cards v2.dc.html`, `image-slot.js`) with "针对我页面中NCAA Bridge中 1, What the model says修改" (apply this to the NCAA Bridge page's section 1).
- What was used: the handoff's spec implemented against the page's real data — a header row (mono index + serif title, baseline-aligned, 16px gap) and three profile cards in a CSS grid (`repeat(auto-fit, minmax(300px,1fr))`, 24px gap, max-width 1120px), each card carrying a 104px circular headshot with a hairline ring, serif name, mono meta line, a 150px donut of the top-3 archetype weights with the remainder as "Others", and a legend. Replaces the three horizontal Plotly bar charts that were there. The donut is a pure CSS `conic-gradient` with a card-coloured hole — no charting library and no JS — so it renders through `st.markdown` without an iframe and without the height-juggling every `st.iframe` table on this app needs.
- Judgment calls, each made against something real rather than assumed: (a) **Colour** — the handoff is explicit that its cream/amber palette is placeholder and that colours must be mapped by ROLE to the host app's tokens; mapped paper→BL_PAPER, card→BL_WHITE, ink→BL_INK, muted→BL_MUTED, hairline→BL_LINE, ramp-1/2/3→three tones of this app's primary green (dark→light, preserving the handoff's rank-by-darkness encoding), others→BL_INK at 10%. The green ramp is the same one `step2_intro.py` already uses to mark projected recipes, so "modelled, not measured" now reads consistently across both pages. (b) **Type** — the handoff names Newsreader/IBM Plex Mono but defers to "the app's existing font stack if it has one"; loading two webfonts would add an external network dependency to a page that has none, so the serif/mono *contrast* the design depends on is kept via system stacks instead of the named families. (c) **Headshots** — the handoff ships no assets and its mock uses drag-in placeholders; checked the three rookies' `nba_player_id`s from `config.yaml` and confirmed all three resolve to **real NBA photos**, so the cards use those (the shared helper degrades to an initials avatar on its own if one ever goes missing). (d) **Markup** — used `<div>`s rather than `<h1>/<h2>` for the title and names, because this app's global CSS forces `font-weight:700 !important` on every heading tag and would silently override the design's 600.
- Content preserved, not dropped: the per-card "closest real comparison" list and the comps table moved into a per-rookie expander directly beneath each card (aligned via `st.columns`), so the cards carry the answer and the evidence stays one click away. "Others" is computed as `100 − sum(top-3)` from the same rounded integers the arcs use, so the legend always sums to exactly 100.
- Verified: the rendered values match the handoff's own data table **exactly** for all three players (Brown 55/11/10 + 24 Others, Bilodeau 35/30/8 + 27, Jefferson 24/23/20 + 33) — worth noting that Bilodeau's 35% only matches because Entry 142 fixed the double-rounding that previously rendered it as 34% in this exact spot. Live-browser check at 1600px confirmed the grid resolves to three equal 357px columns capped at exactly 1120px as specified, and at 1280px it auto-wraps to two columns, matching the handoff's stated responsive behaviour. Structural test updated (Act 1's heading is now a rendered header row, not a markdown string — the existing test caught this correctly on first run). Full suite 131/131 passing.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the entire design — layout, type scale, spacing, the donut-with-Others encoding, the rank-by-darkness rule, and the decision to apply it to this section — all the owner's, supplied as the handoff.

## Entry 144 — NCAA Bridge section 1: 8-archetype radar, profile-left/chart-right rows (src/step5_rookie_projections.py)

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "把三个卡片铺开 并且针对显示球员receipt 我希望画这个图像 然后因为我们一共有8个纬度 在8个纬度显示球员的能力 并且前三的纬度使用加粗更大字体的展示这个receipt 然后我希望 profile + chart 是左右排列显示 不是上下的" (spread the three cards out; to show the player's recipe I want this chart; we have 8 dimensions, show the player's ability across all 8; set the top 3 dimensions in bold and a larger font; and I want profile + chart side by side, not stacked) — supplied with a screenshot of this app's own signature radar as the visual reference.
- What was used: the three-across donut grid (Entry 143) replaced by one full-width row per rookie — profile panel on the left (headshot, serif name, mono meta, recipe legend), an 8-axis radar on the right, and that rookie's comps expander directly beneath his own row. The radar follows the conventions of the existing `step3_player_breakdown.signature_radar_chart` the reference screenshot came from: `Scatterpolar`, loop closed by repeating the first category, BL_GREEN line with a translucent fill, BL_PAPER polar background, and `categoryorder="array"` — that last one is not cosmetic, step3's own comment records that Plotly silently regroups a categorical angular axis once the first category is repeated to close the loop. Top-3 emphasis is applied in both places it can be read: the radar's axis labels (bold, 14px vs 11.5px — the category string doubles as the tick label, so the HTML lives in the string itself, verified to survive into the figure) and the legend rows (17px/600 vs 14.5px/400, with a larger swatch).
- Two choices the prompt did not specify, both made so the three radars can actually be COMPARED rather than merely coexist: the 8 axes are in **fixed archetype order for every player** (sorting each by his own weights would put a different archetype at 12 o'clock on each chart, making the shapes look comparable while meaning nothing), and the **radial range is shared across all three** (computed as the ceiling-to-10 of the largest weight among the rookies, 60%), so a 55% lobe on one chart sits the same distance from centre as a 55% lobe on another. With both in place the charts read against each other directly: Brown is a single spike (55% concentrated), Bilodeau a two-lobe split (35/30), Jefferson a broad low shape (24/23/20).
- Also fixed while verifying live: the radial tick labels sit over the filled shape, and at Plotly's default interval they crowded the very lobes they were there to measure — set to `dtick=20` (three rings) at a smaller size. The exact values are in the legend beside the chart regardless.
- One self-inflicted mistake worth recording: the first patch attempt replaced a source range whose two anchors bracketed `render_section1_projections` as well, so it would have deleted that function; the script asserted on the next anchor and raised BEFORE writing, so nothing was corrupted. Redone as a single explicit block replacement and verified by listing the resulting function names (no duplicates, no survivors of the old donut path).
- Verified: programmatic checks on all three figures — 8 axes each, loop closed, exactly 3 bold labels per player and those 3 matching that player's own top-3, identical axis ORDER across all three players, and identical `range=(0, 60)`. Profile panel confirmed donut-free with top-3 emphasised and Others not. Live-browser screenshots at 1600px confirmed the left/right layout and the decluttered rings. Full suite 131/131 passing.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the radar itself, the 8-dimension framing, the bold/larger treatment for the top 3, the profile-left/chart-right layout, and the instruction to spread the cards out — all given directly by the owner.

- The accuracy answer, computed from the frozen holdout at render time rather than asserted: (a) n=36 makes the 95% bootstrap interval on top-1 run **36%–67%**, a 31-point span that also covers the 41.7% league-average baseline — so JSD (0.169 vs 0.243) is the sturdier evidence, and I checked how the project's own `beats_t5i` flag is set (a JSD point comparison in `phase5_step2_metrics.py:110`, not a paired significance test) so the page clarifies rather than contradicts it; (b) errors are **not** evenly spread — *3&D Wing* is the weakest class (33% on n=9) and the single most common miss is 3&D Wing → Combo Guard (×3), two perimeter roles separated mainly by shot location and play type, i.e. exactly the 16-of-29 dimensions college data lacks, which turns the abstract missing-data caveat into a nameable confusion; (c) the model **hedges** — mean real peak 0.468 vs mean predicted peak 0.335, systematically 13pp flatter, and the page is explicit that sharpening would improve JSD and the intervals but would **not** move top-1, since rescaling leaves the argmax unchanged; (d) hit rate tracks how clear-cut the truth is (38% hybrid / 52% moderate / 80% clear-cut), which reframes part of the miss rate as a property of the question. Improvement levers stated in priority order: more anchors first (237 is the ceiling on everything else), the missing college dimensions second (a data-acquisition problem no tuning fixes), calibration cheaply, and — before blaming the model — a split-half reliability check on the rookie recipe itself, the same test this project already used to settle the archetype-RAPM question.

Every span in that card now carries an explicit font-size. Also normalized a display-only double space in a few comp names ("Antonio  Reeves") from the source data.
- Verified: `holdout_error_profile()` output checked directly (n=36, hit 52.8%, peaks 0.468/0.335, bands 38/52/80%, weakest class 3&D Wing 33% on n=9) and confirmed identical to what the page renders; live-browser screenshots of both rebuilt acts and the three compact cards with an opened comps expander. Structural test updated for the renamed acts and extended to require the two new render functions. Full suite 131/131 passing.
- What was NOT AI-generated: both re-designs (the compact 3-across layout, chart-under-profile, comps-as-cards showing the transformation, dropping the table and the explanatory paragraph) and the re-partition of acts 2 and 3 — all the owner's own, given directly. ## Entry 152 — Widen the NCAA Bridge player browser from 36 to 273, out-of-sample (src/translator/phase5_step4_oof_recipes.py NEW, src/step5_rookie_projections.py, tests/)

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "只选择2025太少了 选择更多 please" (only being able to pick 2025 is too few — offer more).
- The blocking question, resolved before writing any UI: more players is only honest if their projections are out-of-sample. `anchors.csv` already holds all 273 players' college recipe (`c_alpha_*`) and true rookie recipe (`y_*`), but the predicted vector existed only for the 36 held-out 2025 players. `cv_harness.py` does compute leave-one-draft-class-out predictions for the other 237 — it just discards the vectors after reducing them to scalars (jsd/l1/top1_hit). The tempting shortcut, running the deployment model over those 237, would be **in-sample** (it was trained on them) and would flatter the model precisely where a reader checks it, so it was rejected.
- What was used: a new `src/translator/phase5_step4_oof_recipes.py` that re-runs the chosen cell alone (T1, variant b) across the 8 folds and persists the vectors — measured at ~10s/fold, ~85s total, so this was cheap enough to just do rather than approximate. Seeded identically to `cv_harness` (seed = fold index) so it reproduces that run instead of being a second, subtly different one, and the script **verifies** that by re-deriving every player's JSD from the new vectors and comparing against the stored CV metrics: n=237, max|Δjsd| = 4.3e-08. The portal then reads both sources through one `load_browsable_players()` loader that keeps their provenance in an explicit `basis` column rather than silently pooling them, and the page states that basis under each player ("no model that saw this player produced it").
- Sanity check that the pooling is sound: top-1 hit over all 273 is 51.6%, and the 2025 subset alone reproduces **exactly 52.8%** — the published holdout figure — so the added 237 neither shifted nor contaminated the number the project already reports.
- Also expanded the sortable table to the same 273-player pool, and fixed a caption that still said "Pick a held-out 2025 draftee" after the pool widened.
- Verified: two new tests — one asserts the browsable pool is entirely out-of-sample (2025 must carry the held-out basis and 2017-2024 the CV basis, both recipes valid simplexes, one row per player), so a future change that quietly swaps in in-sample fitted values fails loudly; the other asserts the regenerated vectors still reproduce the stored CV run to <1e-6. Live-browser confirmation of the 273-player selector. Full suite 134/134.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the request for more players. The decision to generate genuine leave-one-draft-class-out vectors rather than reuse in-sample predictions, and to record the provenance per player, was this session's — made because the cheap path would have quietly overstated the model.

## Entry 155 — Precompute league role-elasticity spreads (src/pipeline/precompute_elasticity_spreads.py NEW, src/portal_shared.py, tests/)

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "部署成功了 Computing role-elasticity spreads league-wide (about a minute, cached after)... 但是这一部分在我打开正式页面的时候要加载很长时间".
- Diagnosed before changing anything: the loop spends **54s to produce 380 floats (3KB)**. Profiled the parts rather than guessing — the parquet read is 0.07s and OS-cached, so the cost is the per-player derivation (~0.125s × 433), not I/O. Streamlit's cache makes it once-per-container, but Community Cloud recycles containers often enough that real visitors kept paying the full minute.
- Two false alarms checked and dismissed before reporting them, which is why the fix stayed small: (a) the committed `stints_2025_26.parquet` lacks the process-metric columns the elasticity code requires — but `_load_season_stints_v2_or_v1` prefers `stints_2025_26_v2.parquet`, which **is** committed and does have them, so the deployed numbers were correct all along; (b) my uncommitted local parquet differs from the committed one, but the shared columns are byte-identical (verified against the git blob), so precomputing locally cannot ship numbers the deployed data would not reproduce.
- What was used: the derivation extracted into `compute_league_elasticity_spreads()` and precomputed by a new script into `data/league_elasticity_spreads_2025_26.json` (8KB). The loader reads the artifact and **keeps the live computation as a fallback**, so a checkout that never ran the script still works — slowly, exactly as before — rather than failing. One implementation is shared by the script and the fallback; a second copy would be free to drift and silently change the percentile it feeds. The artifact records its own season and k and is ignored if either disagrees, because a stale file answering for the wrong basis would move every elasticity percentile on the page with nothing on screen to show it.
- Verified: **50.8s → 2.3ms (~21,000×)**, arrays bit-identical (max difference 0.0), and the percentiles the page actually renders unchanged at three probe values including the 6.9pp figure quoted in the README for Michael Porter Jr. Test added asserting the artifact is well-formed, non-negative, self-consistent, and that the loader's k/season gate is live; the full 50s equality check was done by hand once rather than added to every test run. Full suite 135/135.
- What was NOT AI-generated: noticing the load time on the deployed page — the owner's.

## Entry 158 — Stop highlighting everyone in the league-wide hull view (src/step2_intro.py, src/hull_callout_chart.py)

- The filter already existed — the sidebar team picker drives this page. The real defect was one I introduced in the league-wide conversion: with "All 30 teams" selected, the highlight set became every player, so the chart rendered **425 dark dots against 8 grey ones**. Highlighting is a "this team against the league" device; applied to everyone it is not a weaker version of itself, it is the absence of it. Measured before changing anything rather than guessing at the cause.

- Latent bug this surfaced: passing an empty highlight set crashed `build_figure_spec` with a `ValueError` from `np.vstack`, because the empty list comprehension yields shape `(0,)` and cannot stack against the `(n,2)` vertex array. "Nobody highlighted" is a legitimate state, not an error, so the fix reshapes to `(0,2)` rather than special-casing the caller. Without this the clean league view was simply unreachable.
- Also fixed prose that went stale in the same conversion: the intro sentence still read "black dots are Nets players" while the league view shows no dark dots at all — it described something not on screen. The sentence now follows the selection ("the cloud is every player who logged 300+ minutes this season" league-wide, "the dark dots are LAL players" per team).
- Verified: built figure specs directly to count highlighted vs background points in each mode; live AppTest confirming the caption text tracks all three selection states; browser screenshot of the cleaned league view. Full suite 135/135.

## Entry 164 — Faces IN the 3D scene (three.js); drop the "similar players" strip (src/hull_3d_chart.py NEW, src/pipeline/precompute_player_thumbnails.py NEW, data/player_thumbs_2025_26.json NEW, src/step2_intro.py)

- Date: 2026-08-25
- Tool: Claude Code
- Prompt: "我想在3D图像上显示这个球员头像 不需要在下面单独加这么一个段落：Who else fits each type? 而且我觉得当我用波轮扩大这个图像内部的时候 那些黑点应该也显示球员 这样会不会更好呢".
- Plotly's scatter3d cannot put an image on a 3D marker, so this replaces the Plotly 3D chart with a from-scratch three.js scene where every player is a billboard sprite - a circular headshot that always faces the camera, staying legible through rotation and zoom. The 8 archetype corners are always headshot sprites with a colored ring and the player's name; the ~425 others are a colored point cloud that becomes headshot sprites once the camera zooms in past a distance threshold (2.7), so scrolling into the interior reveals who each dot is - exactly the owner's suggestion. Faint axes + a bounding box give spatial reference; a Reset button restores the default framing; hover shows a face+name+archetype tooltip. The "Who else fits each type?" strip was removed as asked - hovering/zooming the scene now IS how you see who fits where.
- The load-bearing constraint solved: WebGL rejects cross-origin textures without CORS and the NBA CDN sends none, so faces can't be linked - they're embedded as same-origin data URIs. A new precompute (precompute_player_thumbnails.py) turns the 433 cached headshots into 72px circular WebP data URIs (~0.8KB each; all 433 already cached, so no network) and writes data/player_thumbs_2025_26.json (1.2MB), which also makes the faces work on Streamlit Cloud regardless of which full headshots are committed. The payload builder and thumbnail load are @st.cache_data so a rerun doesn't recompute the projection or re-read the 1.2MB file.
- Verified the headline behaviours in a real browser, not just by reasoning: the 8 corner faces render with rings/names; and - by temporarily raising the zoom threshold so faces show at the default camera - confirmed all ~425 cloud dots do become their own headshots (the same faceSprite path the corners use), then restored the threshold to 2.7 so the default view is dots + corner faces and faces appear on zoom-in. Full suite 135/135.
- What was NOT AI-generated: the idea - faces on the 3D corners, and dots that reveal players when you zoom in - the owner's own.
